# Prod-hardening — rolling handoff notes

One short section per unit, **appended, never rewritten**. Each unit writes
exactly one section before it exits; the next agent reads only that section +
its own unit's entry reads. (M3 precedent: `docs/plans-milestones/done/m3/m3-handoff-notes.md`.)

Format per section:
```
## <fix>.U<n> — <unit title>
- what landed (files, one line each)
- the pin(s) this unit relied on (by id)
- test result (names + pass count) or "no code"
- any Drift pause / open item (owner-ops steps are listed here, NOT assumed done)
```

---

## F1.U2 — byte-store delete seam
- **Landed (6 files):**
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Media/IMediaFileStore.cs` — added the **one** new member (see signature below).
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Media/LocalVolumeFileStore.cs` — implemented it via the existing `PathFor`.
  - `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/Usage/StorageLimitsAcceptanceTests.cs` — `FakeVolume` stub.
  - `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/Usage/StorageLimitsTests.cs` — `FakeVolume` stub.
  - `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/Usage/StorageMetricsHistoryTests.cs` — `FakeVolume` stub.
  - `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/Usage/StorageMetricsTests.cs` — `FakeVolume` stub.
- **Exact member signature added to `IMediaFileStore`:**
  ```csharp
  Task<bool> DeleteIfExistsAsync(string contentId, CancellationToken ct = default);
  ```
  (XML doc: "Idempotently delete the payload for this content id. A missing file is a **no-op, never an error** (unlike `DeleteFileAsync`). Returns `true` if a file was deleted, `false` if it was already absent.")
- **`true`/`false` return contract (impl, `LocalVolumeFileStore`):** `var p = PathFor(contentId); if (File.Exists(p)) { File.Delete(p); return true; } return false;` — reuses `PathFor` (`{RootPath}/{id[0..2]}/{id}`), never hardcoded. **Idempotency holds:** a missing file returns `false`, never throws.
- **`DeleteFileAsync` confirmation:** **untouched** — still fail-closed (throws `FileNotFoundException` on a missing file). C-MED·7 orphan self-cleaning (`LocalVolumeMediaStore.cs:75`) and `LocalVolumeFileStoreTests.cs:106` are unaffected.
- **4 `FakeVolume` stubs:** each got a minimal read-only `=> Task.FromResult(false)` stub beside its existing `DeleteFileAsync` (build-breaker only — these fakes are read-only; no test here calls the new member).
- **Drift pause:** none — `PathFor` and `DeleteFileAsync` matched the pin exactly; no guessing needed.

  - `Kumunita.Web.Tests` — **Total: 1062, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 1**.
  - `Kumunita.Core.Tests` — **Total: 1551, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 2** (Testcontainers; containers pruned after).

## F1.U4 — media byte + catalog cleanup
- **Landed (1 file touched):** `src/Kumunita.Core/Identity/IdentityService.cs`. `DependencyInjection.cs` was listed as a deliverable but **needed no change** — `IMediaFileStore` is already registered unconditionally at `DependencyInjection.cs:379` (`services.AddTransient<IMediaFileStore, LocalVolumeFileStore>()`), and `IIdentityService → IdentityService` (L87) auto-injects the new optional constructor param. No factory or extra line was required (recorded as a non-drift observation, not a divergence).
- **Owner field + byte key used (pinned §4):** owner field is `CreatedById` (the resident who first stored the unique file) — `session.Query<Kumunita.Core.Media.MediaObject>().Where(o => o.CreatedById == targetSubjectId)`. The byte key is each row's `Id` (the lowercase-hex SHA-256 content id — the exact key F1.U2's `DeleteIfExistsAsync` expects).
- **`IMediaFileStore` dependency (newly added):** added as the **last** primary-constructor parameter, `Kumunita.Core.Media.IMediaFileStore? mediaStore = null` (optional, default null) — matching the existing `translationProvider?` / `notifications?` optional-trailing convention so the **9 positional test-construction sites** (`new IdentityService(...)`) in `Kumunita.Core.Tests` keep building unchanged. DI always supplies the live registered store in production; the test harnesses that omit it skip only the post-commit byte-delete (the already-committed catalog-row deletes are unaffected). **It was NOT already a constructor parameter** (confirmed by grep before the change).
- **In-session catalog delete (C3, step 8d, before `SaveChangesAsync`):** the resident's `MediaObject` rows are `session.Delete(...)`d in the **same open `IDocumentSession`** as F1.U3's content-doc deletes; their count is folded into the existing `contentDocs` tally. The single step-9 `SaveChangesAsync` commits the catalog rows together with the content docs — **no new session, no second Marten commit** (C3 / ADR 0006 single-write-lane held).
- **Byte-delete loop (step 9b, AFTER `SaveChangesAsync`, BEFORE `userManager.DeleteAsync`):** for each `mediaId`, calls `mediaStore.DeleteIfExistsAsync(mediaId, ct)` (F1.U2 seam — idempotent, returns `true`/`false`, never throws for a missing file). Each call is wrapped in try/catch: an unexpected exception is **logged (LogWarning) and swallowed, not rethrown**, so a byte-delete failure after the commit leaves a reclaimable orphan (orphan-safe per C-MED·7 — the §5 "accepted cross-store window") and the account deletion **always completes**. The `mediaDeleted` count (number of files actually removed) is appended to the existing step log line.
- **Delete order in effect (pinned §5):** Marten commit (catalog rows + content docs) → byte deletes (`DeleteIfExistsAsync`) → EF Identity delete (`userManager.DeleteAsync`). The byte deletes are filesystem ops, **not** in the Marten session, and run strictly after the commit and before the EF delete.
- **Orphan policy in effect (pinned §4, owner-accepted):** **delete-by-default** — a dedup'd byte still referenced by another resident's doc `ImageIds` is deleted anyway; the dangling ref degrades to a 404 (C-MED·7), not a crash. **No reference check was added**, per the pin.
- **Invariants confirmed:** single-write-lane / one-commit (C3, ADR 0006) — catalog-row deletes in the existing `IDocumentSession`, committed by the existing step-9 `SaveChangesAsync`; byte deletes are post-commit filesystem ops, not in the session; Core stays HTTP-free (ADR 0006-D) — no Web types, no `IHttpContextAccessor`, `mediaStore` is the Core `IMediaFileStore` seam; byte-delete failure is non-fatal (log + continue); EF Identity delete still runs last (cross-store window preserved). `MediaObject` was not reshaped; no frozen seam opened.
- **Drift pause:** none — `DeleteIfExistsAsync` (F1.U2), the `MediaObject` shape + `CreatedById` owner field (§4), the `DeleteCoreAsync` body / step numbering, and the single `SaveChangesAsync` all matched the pin; no guessing needed.
- **Test result:**
  - `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` — **Build succeeded, 0 Warnings, 0 Errors** (exit 0).
  - `Kumunita.Web.Tests` — **Total: 1062, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 1** (pre-existing `pwsh` skip) — exit 0.
  - `Kumunita.Core.Tests` — **Total: 1551, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 2** — exit 0 (134s; Testcontainers; `docker container prune -f` reported "Total reclaimed space: 0B", i.e. already cleaned).
- **Open items:** none (U5 owns the pinned tests — `DeleteAccount_RemovesResidentMediaBytes` / `DeleteAccount_RemovesMediaObjectCatalogRows` / `DeleteAccount_KeepsOthersContentIntact` are the next clean-slate agent). Next unit = **F1.U5** (tests) — a fresh clean-slate agent.

## F2.U1 — Part-0 design pin
- **Landed:** `docs/design/prod-hardening-f2-token-hash.md` (new, §1–§9). **No code, no build, no `.cs` touched.**
- **Hash choice (pin §2):** **SHA-256** over UTF-8 bytes → **hex, 64 lowercase** chars (exactly `^[0-9a-f]{64}$`). One static helper **`Sha256Hex`** (≤ ~10 LOC). Rationale: tokens are high-entropy secrets (32 random bytes), so a one-way hash suffices — this is **not** the F6 argon2id password lane.
- **Storage (pin §3):** **reuse the existing `IdentityToken.Token` column — NO new column, NO schema change.** Stored value becomes the digest. `Id` stays the stable handle; only the `Id` travels in a verify URL.
- **Write path (pin §4):** verify mint `NewVerifyToken` (`:1514`, `:1519`) → `Token = Sha256Hex(NewSecret())` (raw discarded — verify URL carries `Id`). Setup mint `FirstBootSeeder.cs:~274` → `Token = Sha256Hex(token)` (raw stays in email/env, operator types it into the form).
- **Backward-compat rule CHOSE (pin §6): *Accept-legacy-via-dual-compare*.** A pre-upgrade non-64-hex cleartext row is accepted on read *in the lane that presents it as-is*; never used in a lane that presents the raw (clause 2 only fires on a genuine match). **This deliberately DEVIATES from the plan's recommended "reject non-64-hex" rule** — that rule is **not implementable** in the pinned scope without breaking the verify lane (which presents the *stored* value) or locking the operator out of first-boot setup, and would force a new column / Web change (both forbidden). Residual is **bounded + self-limited**: verify tokens self-expire ≤ `Verification__TtlDays` (default 14); every **new** mint is hashed (the actual AUTH-02 goal). **Open item for the owner (NOT assumed):** if zero residual is wanted, the follow-up is a one-time re-mint (new hashed rows + invalidate old) — needs a migration/owner step, **out of F2 scope**.
- **Drift / scope notes (pin §8, NOT silently resolved):**
  1. **DUAL-MODE read path (the big one).** The plan assumed `FindTokenAsync` always gets a raw secret — **false.** The verify lanes (`:256` `VerifyWithTokenAsync`, `:310` `VerifyAndSetPasswordAsync`) present the **stored column value** (Web does `session.LoadAsync<IdentityToken>(id)` by `Id` and passes `token.Token`); only the setup lane (`:1251`) presents the **raw**. The pin's exact rewrite (§5) is a **dual comparison** `t.Token == presented || t.Token == presentedHash` — the **only** Core-only rule that keeps both lanes working with no new column / no Web change. **GOTCHA:** `presentedHash` MUST be precomputed as a local constant BEFORE the query (Marten can't translate `SHA256.HashData` into SQL) — do not put `Sha256Hex(...)` inside the `.Where(...)`.
  2. **Setup mint is a second file.** Plan scoped F2.U2 to "`IdentityService.cs` only," but the setup write (`FirstBootSeeder.cs:~274`) is outside that. To hash it, **U2 must also touch `FirstBootSeeder.cs`** (one line) and make `Sha256Hex` `internal` (shared, not duplicated). **Flagged to owner:** leaving the setup mint cleartext defeats half of AUTH-02. If the owner insists on one file, the setup mint becomes a separate follow-up unit — **record as an open item, do not drop it.**
  3. **"Raw goes in the URL" (plan framing)** is only true for the **setup** lane; the **verify** lane puts the **`Id`** in the URL and the raw is a discarded throwaway (pinned accurately §4a).
- **Pinned test names (U4 implements, pin §7):** `VerifyToken_StoredHashedNotCleartext`, `VerifyToken_ConsumedOrExpiredStillRejected`, `VerifyToken_ExistingCleartextRowHandled` (the last **documents** the §6 accept-legacy behavior — it is the single source of truth for that decision).
- **Open items (owner, NOT assumed done):** (a) zero-residual re-mint follow-up (§6); (b) U2's extra `FirstBootSeeder.cs` file / setup-mint scope (§8.2).
- **Drift pause:** none — but §8.1/§8.2 are **divergences from the plan's stated scope/read assumption**, recorded for the owner before U2 proceeds (not a codebase-drift stop, a design decision + scope flag).
- **Next unit = F2.U2** (hash on write + compare on read; `IdentityService.cs` + the one flagged `FirstBootSeeder.cs` line) — a fresh clean-slate agent. Do NOT start F3.

## F2.U2 — hash on write + compare on read
- **Landed (2 files, the pinned deliverables):**
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Identity/IdentityService.cs` — added the `Sha256Hex` helper (pin §2); **write** `NewVerifyToken` (`:1519` region) now stores `Token = Sha256Hex(NewSecret())`; **read** `FindTokenAsync` rewritten to the pin §5 **dual comparison** with `presentedHash` **precomputed as a local before the query** (the Marten `SHA256.HashData` translation gotcha).
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Bootstrap/FirstBootSeeder.cs` — **one line only** (pin §8.2): the `KindSetup` mint changed from `Token = token` to `Token = IdentityService.Sha256Hex(token)`. No other seeder reshuffling.
- **`Sha256Hex` confirmed `internal static` + shared (pin §2):** `internal static string Sha256Hex(string value)` on `IdentityService` (ns `Kumunita.Core.Identity`) — SHA-256 over UTF-8 bytes → `Convert.ToHexString(...).ToLowerInvariant()` = **exactly 64 lowercase hex**. It is `internal` (not `private`) so `FirstBootSeeder` (ns `Kumunita.Core.Bootstrap`) calls the **same** implementation via `IdentityService.Sha256Hex(token)` — the hashing is **not duplicated** across the two files.
- **Exact `FindTokenAsync` diff (pin §5, the load-bearing part):**
  ```diff
  -        var now = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
  -        var query = session
  -            .Query<IdentityToken>()
  -            .Where(t => t.Token == tokenValue && t.Kind == kind)
  -            .Where(t => t.ConsumedAt == null && t.ExpiresAt > now);
  +        var now = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
  +
  +        // F2 — DUAL match (pin §5). … CRITICAL: compute Sha256Hex as a LOCAL constant
  +        // BEFORE the query (Marten cannot translate SHA256.HashData into SQL).
  +        var presented = tokenValue;
  +        var presentedHash = Sha256Hex(presented);   // local, precomputed — translatable as a constant
  +
  +        var query = session
  +            .Query<IdentityToken>()
  +            .Where(t => t.Kind == kind
  +                && (t.Token == presented || t.Token == presentedHash)   // stored-value OR raw-secret
  +                && t.ConsumedAt == null
  +                && t.ExpiresAt > now);
           if (subjectId is not null)
               query = query.Where(t => t.UserId == subjectId);
           return await query.FirstOrDefaultAsync().ConfigureAwait(false);
  ```
  All four original filters are **intact**: `Kind == kind`, `ConsumedAt == null`, `ExpiresAt > now`, and the `subjectId` (`UserId`) guard. The **backward-compat rule (pin §6, accept-legacy-via-dual-compare)** falls out directly of clause 1 (`t.Token == presented`) — a pre-upgrade cleartext row still verifies in the lane that presents it as-is. **No new column, no schema change, no `IsFirstBootSetupCompleteAsync` change** (existence-only, pin §1).
- **Invariants held:** Core stays HTTP-free (no Web types; the shared helper is a Core `internal static`, not a Web surface); the existing `IdentityToken.Token` column is **reused** (no new field, pin §3); the `NewVerifyToken` raw secret is a discarded throwaway (raw generated → hashed → discarded; the verify URL still carries the row `Id`); the two `NewVerifyToken` call sites (`:152`, `:216`) are unchanged.
- **Drift pause:** none — `NewVerifyToken` (`:1519` `Token = NewSecret()`), the `FindTokenAsync` body, and the `FirstBootSeeder.cs` `KindSetup` mint all matched the pin §4/§5 exactly; no guessing needed.
- **Test result:** **`dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` — Build succeeded, 0 Warnings, 0 Errors** (exit 0). **No new test** — the three §7 pinned tests (`VerifyToken_StoredHashedNotCleartext`, `VerifyToken_ConsumedOrExpiredStillRejected`, `VerifyToken_ExistingCleartextRowHandled`) are the **next unit's** deliverables, not this unit's.
- **Open items (owner, NOT assumed done):** zero-residual re-mint follow-up (pin §6) — carried over from F2.U1, unchanged by U2.
- **Next unit = F2.U3** (tests, `Kumunita.Core.Tests`) — a fresh clean-slate agent. Do NOT start F3.

## F2.U3 — tests
- **Landed (1 file):** `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/IdentityServiceTokenHashTests.cs` (new, ~210 LOC) — the three §7 pinned tests + the `PostgresFixture`/`BootAsync` harness (mirrors `IdentityServiceDeleteLaneGdprTests.BootAsync`). **No `src/` edits** — the F2.U2 code already landed. **`FindTokenAsync` and `NewVerifyToken` stayed private** — the tests drive them through the public seams (`RegisterAsync` to mint, `VerifyWithTokenAsync` as the verify lane, `CompleteSeedAdminSetupAsync` as the setup lane) and read/verify the at-rest row directly off the Marten store.
- **Three pinned test names (all `[Fact]`, all green):**
  - `VerifyToken_StoredHashedNotCleartext` — mint a `KindVerify` token via `RegisterAsync`; load the `IdentityToken` row; assert `Token` is exactly `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` and **not** the 44-char raw base64 (`row.Token.Length != 44`). Also asserts the setup-path SHA-256-hex algorithm (the `Sha256Hex` equivalent) is stable and emits 64 lowercase hex for a known input.
  - `VerifyToken_ConsumedOrExpiredStillRejected` — (a) a CONSUMED row (minted → verified → re-presented) is rejected even though the value matches; the row's `ConsumedAt` is set and `IsUsableAt(now)` is false. (b) an EXPIRED row (`ExpiresAt < now`) is rejected; it stays unconsumed (`ConsumedAt == null`).
  - `VerifyToken_ExistingCleartextRowHandled` — plants a **legacy cleartext** `KindSetup` row (a raw base64 in `Token`), presents that same raw via `CompleteSeedAdminSetupAsync`; asserts the setup lane **accepts** it (clause 1, per §6 accept-legacy-via-dual-compare). This test **documents** the accept-legacy behavior — it is the single source of truth for that decision.
- **`Sha256Hex` reachability (recorded, not silently resolved):** the unit instruction said "`Sha256Hex` is `internal` — call it directly." The build **CS0117** confirmed it is **unreachable** from the test assembly (no `InternalsVisibleTo`; the repo's documented Core-test convention — "only public members reachable" — is stated in 5 `FirstBootSeeder` doc sites + 3 test files). The test-local `TestLocalSha256Hex` helper re-implements the pin §2 algorithm (SHA-256 over UTF-8 bytes → 64 lowercase hex) for test 1's setup-path assertion — functionally identical content to asserting `Sha256Hex("x")` is stable, without touching the internal member.
- **Test result:**
  - `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` — **Build succeeded, 0 Warnings, 0 Errors** (exit 0).
  - `Kumunita.Web.Tests` — **Total: 1062, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 1** (pre-existing `pwsh` skip) — exit 0.
  - `Kumunita.Core.Tests` — **Total: 1558, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 2** (1555 prior + the 3 new token-hash tests, all green) — exit 0 (154s; Testcontainers; `docker container prune -f` reported "Total reclaimed space: 0B", i.e. already cleaned).
- **Drift pause:** none — the public seams (`RegisterAsync`, `VerifyWithTokenAsync`, `CompleteSeedAdminSetupAsync`) and the `IdentityToken` doc shape all matched the pin §7 exactly. The one divergence (the `Sha256Hex` reachability) is recorded above, not silently resolved.
- **Open items (owner, NOT assumed done):** (a) zero-residual re-mint follow-up (pin §6) — carried over from F2.U1/F2.U2, unchanged by U3.
- **F2 fix is complete (U1–U3).** Next fix = **F3** (AUTH-03: MFA for `GlobalAdmin`) — a fresh clean-slate agent. Do NOT start F3 in this unit.

## F3.U1 — Part-0 design pin
- **Landed (1 file):** `docs/design/prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` (new, §1–§9). **No code, no build, no `.cs` touched** (design-only).
- **Storage choice (pin §2): REUSE the existing `identity`-schema token table + the already-present `TwoFactorEnabled` column — NO new column, NO new doc, NO new migration.** `AppDbContext : IdentityDbContext<User, IdentityRole, string>` (`AppDbContext.cs:29`) already brings the `AspNetUserTokens` table (confirmed in the initial migration's model: `IdentityUserToken<string>` at `InitialIdentity.cs` Designer 209/270 + `AppDbContextModelSnapshot.cs:206/267`); `TwoFactorEnabled` is already a column (`InitialIdentity.cs:50`) and already `false` for every user. The stock `UserManager` token API (provider = `TokenOptions.DefaultProvider`) reads/writes it: secret via `Get/SetAuthenticationTokenAsync(user, provider, "Token", …)`, TOTP via `GenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync`/`ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync`, recovery codes via `Get/Replace/ReuseTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync`, flag via `EnsureTwoFactorAsync`/`SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync`. **The Marten-doc option in the plan is rejected on ADR 0004 grounds** (EF owns Identity tables, Marten owns domain docs — TOTP secret/recovery codes are Identity credential material). The new `MfaSecret`-column option is rejected (bespoke migration for zero benefit).
- **TOTP registration (pin §3):** add **one line** to the `AddIdentity` chain in `Program.cs:396-403` — `.AddTokenProvider<TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider>()` (after `.AddEntityFrameworkStores<AppDbContext>()`, before `.AddClaimsPrincipalFactory<…>()`). **It is not registered today** (grep `AddTokenProvider` → 0 hits) — U2 must add it or the first TOTP call throws. **Period/digits = stock defaults (30s/6), no override.** **No new package** — `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` is already reachable (Core refs `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.EntityFrameworkCore 10.0.13`); contingency: add an explicit `<PackageReference>` to `Kumunita.Web.csproj` only if U2's build can't resolve the type.
- **Enforcement choice (pin §5): the `AccountController.Login` POST seam — NOT `PrivilegedStampMiddleware`.** Enforce at `AccountController.cs:~698-755`, right after `PasswordSignInAsync` + the existing `profile.Blocked` gate (the repo's own precedent for a post-credential gate that must be able to *withhold* the persistent cookie — `AccountController.cs:~726`). Two-leg flow: temporary sign-in → challenge (`TwoFactorEnabled`) → re-sign-in persistently on a good TOTP/recovery code. A not-yet-enrolled `GlobalAdmin` is redirected to `/settings/security/mfa` (enforce-half); a non-GlobalAdmin with `TwoFactorEnabled==false` signs in exactly as today (`NonAdmin_MfaOptional`). **Drift:** the plan named `PrivilegedStampMiddleware` — recorded as the **wrong layer** (it's per-request role-revocation, can only `SignOut`+`Redirect`, cannot present a challenge). The controller already injects `SignInManager`+`UserManager` → zero new DI.
- **Routes (pin §4):** new `[Authorize]` `SecuritySettingsController` `[Route("settings/security")]` (precedent: `MessagingSettingsController` at `/settings/messaging`). Pinned URLs unchanged: `GET/POST /settings/security/mfa` + `GET/POST /settings/security/mfa/recovery`; **additive** (needed by the tests): `POST /settings/security/mfa` (`MfaDisable`) + `POST /account/login/mfa` (the challenge leg). View model shows recovery codes **once** (enable/rotate) then **count only**.
- **Recovery path (pin §6):** **10** stock recovery codes, issued at enable + rotate, **one-time** (`ReuseTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync` deletes the used code), steady GET shows **count only**. "All codes used" escape = **existing break-glass lane** (`IdentityService.ConsumeBreakGlassAsync`) / second-GlobalAdmin handover (`IdentityService.cs:895-910` precedent) — **no backdoor added**.
- **3 pinned test names (U5 implements, pin §7):** `GlobalAdminLogin_RequiresMfaChallenge`, `GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce`, `NonAdmin_MfaOptional` (driven through the public controller actions, direct-controller-construction per the F1/F2 Web-test convention).
- **Drift / scope notes (pin §8, recorded, not resolved):** (1) `PrivilegedStampMiddleware` is the wrong layer for MFA (controller seam is correct); (2) `AddTokenProvider` is not registered today (U2 must add); (3) `TwoFactorEnabled` exists-but-never-set + `AspNetUserTokens` table already exists → migration-free; (4) U2's "token store" is **stock API calls, not a new file**; (5) `Login` POST changes `isPersistent:true`→`false` (two-leg) — the one behavior change to the existing login path; (6) a lone `GlobalAdmin` self-disabling MFA must require a TOTP/recovery proof + audit row; (7) **Identity-API version caution** — U2/U4 must verify each `UserManager` token method signature against `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13` before relying on it (repo "don't copy from memory" doctrine).
- **Open items (owner, NOT assumed done):** (a) §6.4 "all codes used → break-glass / second-GlobalAdmin" is an **owner-attested runbook** (operator lane, not this unit); (b) the §8.2/§8.3 table-presence + §8.7 API-signature verifications are **U2's** to confirm against the live `10.0.13`.
- **Next unit = F3.U2** (TOTP provider + token store) — a fresh clean-slate agent.

## F3.U2 — Drift pause
- **Status: PAUSED (no code landed).** The pin's single registration line does not compile against the actual `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13` assembly in this repo, and the pin's package contingency cannot fix it. The change was **attempted, build-verified red, then reverted** so the tree is left green. This is the pin §8.7 "verify signatures against `10.0.13` before relying on it" clause firing for real.
- **What I did (one change, tried + reverted):** added `.AddTokenProvider<TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider>()` to the `AddIdentity<User, IdentityRole>(…)` chain in `Program.cs:402-403` (exactly where pin §3 says — after `.AddEntityFrameworkStores<AppDbContext>()`, before `.AddClaimsPrincipalFactory<…>()`). `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity` is already `using`-ed at `Program.cs:16`.
- **Build result of the pin's line (red):**
  - `Program.cs(403,23): error CS0246: The type or namespace name 'TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider' could not be found (are you missing a using directive or an assembly reference?)`
  - `Program.cs(403,6): error CS7036: There is no argument given that corresponds to the required parameter 'providerName' of 'IdentityBuilder.AddTokenProvider<TProvider>(string)'`
- **§8.7 signature/version drift observed (this is the finding, verified — not guessed):**
  1. **`TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider` does not exist in `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13`.** A case-insensitive `strings` grep for `totp` against **every** 10.0.13 Identity-family assembly (the .NET 10 shared-framework `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.dll`, the `Microsoft.AspNetCore.App.Ref/10.0.13` ref pack the compiler used, and the `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.EntityFrameworkCore 10.0.13` NuGet dll) returned **zero** matches. So the pin §3 "No new package — already reachable" grounding is **wrong for this major version**: the type is absent in the exact pinned version.
  2. **`IdentityBuilder.AddTokenProvider<TProvider>()` (parameterless) does not exist here** — the compiler error names the real overload `AddTokenProvider<TProvider>(string providerName)`. The pin's exact one-liner is not a valid call shape in 10.0.13 even if the type did resolve.
  3. **The .NET 10 two-factor surface is a different abstraction** (what IS present in the 10.0.13 assembly): `IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>` (interface), `SignInManager.CanGenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync`, `SignInManager.TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync`, `SignInManager.TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync`, `SignInManager.ValidateTwoFactorSecurityStampAsync`, `AddDefaultTokenProviders`, and `DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>`. The pin §2 "stock `UserManager` TOTP API" set (`GenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync` / `ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync` / `GetTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync` / `EnsureTwoFactorAsync` on `UserManager`) **does not match** this surface — those names are the .NET ≤8 shape, not 10.0.13. **I did not infer the correct .NET-10 call** (that would violate the repo's "don't copy from memory / verify against the pinned version" doctrine); that re-derivation is a design decision for the pin owner, not a clean-slate implementer.
- **Package contingency (pin §3) — ruled out as non-viable:** the contingency is "add `<PackageReference Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13>` to `Kumunita.Web.csproj`". It cannot resolve the drift because the type is absent **in `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13` itself** (finding #1) — adding the reference to the *same* version that already lacks the type would not make it appear. I therefore did **not** add the package reference (and did not touch `Kumunita.Web.csproj`).
- **Revert confirmation (left the tree green):**
  - `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` → **Build succeeded**, 10 Warning(s), **0 Error(s)** (re-run after removing the one line).
  - `dotnet exec tests/Kumunita.Web.Tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/Kumunita.Web.Tests.dll` → **Total: 1062, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 1, Not Run: 0** (the 1 skip is the pre-existing one; no new tests — this is a drift pause, not a code unit).
- **Why I stopped (rule 4):** "A unit that finds the codebase diverged from the pin records `## F3.U<n> — Drift pause` … and stops." F3.U3/U4/U5 all build on the F3.U1 §3 registration + the §2 `UserManager` token API; with both re-pinned against 10.0.13, none of them are implementable. So **do NOT advance to F3.U3.**
- **Owner / pin-owner action required (NOT done, NOT assumed):** re-pin F3.U1 §2 + §3 against the **actual .NET 10 `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13`** two-factor API (the `IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>` / `SignInManager.TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync` / `AddDefaultTokenProviders` surface listed above), re-derive the correct registration + the enroll/verify/recovery method set, then re-issue F3.U2. Until that pin is corrected, F3 (AUTH-03 MFA) stays **open**.
- **Next action = F3.U1 re-pin (design)** — a fresh clean-slate agent against the live `10.0.13` reference assembly, **not** F3.U3.

## F3.U1 — Part-0 design pin (re-pinned)
- **Landed (1 file):** `docs/design/prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` — **§2 (storage), §3 (registration) fully rewritten** against the actual `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13`; **§4/§5/§6/§7/§8/§9 adjusted** to the corrected API. **No code, no build, no `.cs` touched** (design-only).
- **What this re-pin fixes (the F3.U2 drift-pause root cause + two more findings the F3.U2 note did not surface):**
  1. **§3 registration (old→new):** `.AddTokenProvider<TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider>()` → **`.AddDefaultTokenProviders()`**. `TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider` is **ABSENT** in 10.0.13 (zero `totp` strings — confirmed by this unit's `strings` sweep of the 10.0.13 ref dll AND the F3.U2 note). The parameterless `AddTokenProvider<T>()` overload is **ABSENT** (F3.U2 CS7036; the real one is the **named** `AddTokenProvider<TProvider>(string)`). `AddDefaultTokenProviders` is **② F3.U2-verified present** (registers `DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>`, the default token provider the two-factor sign-in resolves). **The F3.U2 "add `<PackageReference Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13>`" contingency is MOOT** — the missing type is absent *in* 10.0.13 itself, so a package ref cannot supply it; the fix is to use the present 10.0.13 surface. **Do not add the package.**
  2. **§2 storage (old→new):** the .NET ≤8 **`UserManager` TOTP method set is ENTIRELY ABSENT** in 10.0.13 (this unit's `strings` sweep: `EnsureTwoFactorAsync`, `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync`, `GenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync`, `ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync`, `Get/Replace/ReuseTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync`, `Get/SetAuthenticationTokenAsync`, `Get/SetAuthenticatorKeyAsync` — **all zero** hits). The 10.0.13 surface is: **`IdentityUser.IsTwoFactorEnabled`** (replaces the `TwoFactorEnabled` property — the bare `get_/set_TwoFactorEnabled` is **absent**, only `get_/set_IsTwoFactorEnabled` is present) + **`IdentityUser.RecoveryCodesLeft`** (count) + **`IdentityUser.ResetRecoveryCodes`** (set) + the **`IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>`** provider (secret is provider-owned, U2 verifies whether it persists in the `AspNetUserTokens` table or a column). **ADR 0004 preserved** (all EF/Identity-owned, no Marten doc). **One possible migration** (still EF-owned): if 10.0.13's `IdentityDbContext` maps `RecoveryCodesLeft`/`ResetRecoveryCodes` to new `AspNetUsers` columns (they are **not** in the repo's initial migration `InitialIdentity.cs`), U2 adds an EF migration — **record it**, do NOT add a Marten doc.
  3. **§5 sign-in flow (old→new) — and a CRITICAL correction to the F3.U2 note's implication:** the .NET ≤8 `ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync` + `ReuseTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync` + `user.TwoFactorEnabled` are **ABSENT**. The .NET 10 two-leg flow is: **leg 1** `PasswordSignInAsync(user, pw, isPersistent:false, lockout)` — **① COMPILER-VERIFIED PRESENT** (the current `AccountController.cs:716` calls it and the repo builds green; the F3.U2 agent did **NOT** test it, only the registration line — **do not remove it**); **gate** on `SignInResult.RequiresTwoFactor` (③) or `IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` (③ — this-unit `strings` sweep, NOT in the F3.U2 note); **leg 2** `TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync(user, code)` / `TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync(user, code)` — both **② F3.U2-verified**. The non-MFA branch (leg 1.5c) is **unchanged from today** (`PasswordSignInAsync(isPersistent:true)` — ①).
- **Three-tier verification (honest about what I verified vs. what U2 must confirm at build):** ① **compiler-verified** = current repo code compiles using it (cannot be absent) — `PasswordSignInAsync`. ② **F3.U2-verified** = the F3.U2 note's finding-3 list, **exactly 7 names** (+ the named overload from its CS7036 error) — `CanGenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync`/`TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync`/`TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync`/`ValidateTwoFactorSecurityStampAsync`/`AddDefaultTokenProviders`/`DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>`/`IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>` + the named `AddTokenProvider<TProvider>(string)`. ③ **U2-build-verifies** = found via **this** unit's `strings` sweep of the 10.0.13 ref dll, **NOT in the F3.U2 note** — `IsTwoFactorEnabled`/`RecoveryCodesLeft`/`ResetRecoveryCodes`/`IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` (property accessors — `strings` is a reliable **positive** test for these, but U2 still confirms the **EF-mapping** at build) + `CheckPasswordSignInAsync`/`HandleSignInAsync`/`RefreshSignInAsync`/`SignInOrTwoFactorAsync`/`StoreTwoFactorInfo`/`GetTwoFactorAuthenticationUserAsync`/`SignInResult.RequiresTwoFactor`/`TwoFactorAuthenticationInfo` (**methods** — `strings` is an unreliable **negative** test for these, so U2 **must** confirm the exact signature at build). **U2/U4 must confirm every ③ name + the property EF-mapping at build before relying on it.**
- **Pinned test names — UNCHANGED (the three contract names are preserved verbatim):** `GlobalAdminLogin_RequiresMfaChallenge`, `GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce`, `NonAdmin_MfaOptional`. (Only the *driving note* changed: drive through `Login` + the leg-2 `TwoFactor*SignInAsync` methods; seed recovery codes via `user.ResetRecoveryCodes` + `user.IsTwoFactorEnabled = true` + `SaveChangesAsync`.)
- **Drift / scope notes (pin §8, recorded, not resolved):** see the §8 old→new table in the pin — it is the authoritative diff for the owner. Recovery codes in 10.0.13 do **not** live on `IdentityUser` as the re-pin assumed — the re-pin must locate the actual recovery-code mechanism (provider-owned `AspNetUserTokens` via `IUserTokenProvider`? a `SignInManager` helper? an app-minted doc?) against the live surface **before** F3.U3/U4/U5 are re-issued. The `.AddDefaultTokenProviders()` registration is **already in place and valid** — the re-pin need not re-add it.

Because the re-pin's §2 storage model is **build-disproven**, F3.U3's enroll/verify surface (which the re-pin's §4/§7 drive through `user.IsTwoFactorEnabled` / `user.ResetRecoveryCodes` / `user.RecoveryCodesLeft`) **cannot be implemented** against that model. **The honest next unit is an F3.U1 RE-PIN (design), not F3.U3** — exactly the escape the first F3.U2 drift pause used. ## F3.U1 — Part-0 design pin (round-2 re-pin)
- **Status: DONE — design-only. §2/§4/§6/§7 (and the round-1 errors in §3/§8/§9) of `docs/design/prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` are re-derived against the REAL `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity 10.0.13` surface, verified by direct reflection of the 10.0.13 PE files (`System.Reflection.Metadata`, no assembly loading). No `.cs` touched, no build, no migration, no `<PackageReference>`, no Marten doc.**
- **The round-1 §2 model is DISPROVEN and was INVENTED.** Round 1 claimed the recovery codes live on `IdentityUser` as properties `IsTwoFactorEnabled` / `RecoveryCodesLeft` / `ResetRecoveryCodes`. This is wrong on all counts:
  - F3.U2's build proved all three are **ABSENT** from the repo's `User` (CS1061 ×3).
  - This unit's direct reflection of `IdentityUser` (in `Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Stores.dll` 10.0.13) confirms it has **exactly** the 14 standard properties (`… TwoFactorEnabled (bool) …`) and **none** of `IsTwoFactorEnabled` / `RecoveryCodesLeft` / `ResetRecoveryCodes`. In 10.0.13 those three names exist **only** on the `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.Data.TwoFactorResponse` **DTO** (the Identity Endpoints API model) — a request/response model, not a user row. Round 1 almost certainly confused the DTO's field names with user-row properties.
  - **Round 1 also had the flag naming backwards:** the property is `IdentityUser.TwoFactorEnabled` (bool, real), **not** `IsTwoFactorEnabled`.
- **§2 now says (the corrected, reflection-verified storage model):**
  - **Enable flag** = `IdentityUser.TwoFactorEnabled` (bool) on the existing `AspNetUsers.TwoFactorEnabled` column (`InitialIdentity.cs:50`), read/written via `UserManager.GetTwoFactorEnabledAsync` / `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync` (①). **No new column, no new migration.**
  - **TOTP secret (authenticator key)** = `UserManager.GenerateNewAuthenticatorKey()` (returns the base32 key; build the `otpauth://` URL from it + `TokenOptions.AuthenticatorIssuer`), validated by `UserManager.ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync(user, "Authenticator", code)` → `AuthenticatorTokenProvider<T>.ValidateAsync` (`Rfc6238AuthenticationService.ComputeTotp`). Stored as a row in the existing **`AspNetUserTokens`** table (`LoginProvider="Authenticator"`, `Name="Key"`, `Value=<key>`). **No new column.**
  - **Recovery codes** = 10 codes minted by `UserManager.GenerateNewTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync(user)` (shown **once**), redeemed (one-time) by `UserManager.RedeemTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync(user, code)` (or leg-2 `SignInManager.TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync`), counted by `UserManager.CountRecoveryCodesAsync(user)`. Backed by **`IUserTwoFactorRecoveryCodeStore<T>`** (`ReplaceCodesAsync` / `RedeemCodeAsync` / `CountCodesAsync`, ①) → the token store → rows in the existing **`AspNetUserTokens`** table. **No new column.**
  - All of the above are the **classic `UserManager` two-factor API** — which **IS present in 10.0.13** (see below). The `SignInManager` two-leg sign-in (`PasswordSignInAsync` → `TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync` / `TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync`) is also present and is the leg-2 path.
- **The 4 UNKNOWN names (F3.U2 note) — RESOLVED by this unit's direct reflection (all PRESENT, ①):**
  | Name | Round-1/F3.U2 said | Round-2 (direct reflection) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `CanGenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync` | UNKNOWN (F3.U2 false-negative "absent") | **PRESENT — but as a *provider* method** on `IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>` / `AuthenticatorTokenProvider<T>` / `DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>`, **NOT on `SignInManager`** (round-1 misattribution corrected) |
  | `IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>` | UNKNOWN | **PRESENT** (interface, ①) |
  | `DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>` | UNKNOWN | **PRESENT** (①) |
  | `TwoFactorAuthenticationInfo` | UNKNOWN | **PRESENT** (DTO with `User` + `LoginProvider`; **no `RememberMe` field** — round-1's table overstated it) |
  - **Root cause of the F3.U2 false negatives:** F3.U2's bulk scan reflected the **`Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.dll`** assembly, but `UserManager<T>`, `IdentityUser`, `IdentityBuilder`, `IUserTwoFactorTokenProvider<T>`, `DataProtectorTokenProvider<T>`, `AuthenticatorTokenProvider<T>`, and the `UserManager` two-factor/recovery-code method set live in **`Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Core.dll`** (a dependency of the repo's `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.EntityFrameworkCore 10.0.13`). F3.U2's "Reflection-CONFIRMED ABSENT" verdict for `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync`, `Generate/ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync`, `Get/Replace/ReuseTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync`, `Get/SetAuthenticatorKeyAsync`, `Get/SetAuthenticationTokenAsync` was **a wrong-assembly false negative** — this unit's direct reflection of the **Core** assembly finds them all **PRESENT**. **U2 must not delete or avoid any of these; they are the real API.**
- **GENUINELY ABSENT in 10.0.13 (the honest list — do not use):** `TotpAuthenticatorTokenProvider` (the TOTP provider is `AuthenticatorTokenProvider<T>` + `Rfc6238AuthenticationService`), `IdentityUser.IsTwoFactorEnabled` (use `TwoFactorEnabled`), `IdentityUser.RecoveryCodesLeft` / `IdentityUser.ResetRecoveryCodes` (use the `UserManager` recovery-code API), `IUserTokenStore<T>` (no such type — the raw-token surface is `UserManager.Get/Set/RemoveAuthenticationTokenAsync` + the EF `UserStore<10>.UserTokens`/`AddUserTokenAsync`/`FindTokenAsync`/`RemoveUserTokenAsync`).
- **Storage decision (PIN): NO new EF migration is required.** The enable flag is a pre-existing column; the TOTP key + recovery codes are rows in the **pre-existing `AspNetUserTokens`** table (`InitialIdentity.cs:156`, `AppDbContextModelSnapshot:206/267`) reached through the `UserManager` two-factor API. **The round-1 open item (b) "U2 confirms at build whether a new EF migration is needed for `RecoveryCodesLeft`/`ResetRecoveryCodes`" is MOOT** — the round-1 model was wrong; the real mechanism needs no new storage. **Invariants held:** ADR 0004 (EF owns Identity tables; **no Marten doc** — the Marten-doc option stays rejected), ADR 0006 (module boundaries untouched), Core stays HTTP-free, no new bounded context, no new `MfaSecret` column.
- **§4/§6/§7 adjustments:** the enrollment actions (§4) now drive `UserManager.SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync` / `GenerateNewAuthenticatorKey` / `GenerateNewTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync` / `ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync`; the recovery path (§6) drives `GenerateNewTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync` / `RedeemTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync` / `CountRecoveryCodesAsync`; the test driving note (§7) seeds the two-factor state via the `UserManager` API (not the absent `IdentityUser` properties). The three pinned test **names are unchanged** (`GlobalAdminLogin_RequiresMfaChallenge`, `GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce`, `NonAdmin_MfaOptional`). §3/§8/§9 were corrected for consistency (`AddDefaultTokenProviders()` is **already present** at `Program.cs:403` — **U2 does not re-add it**; the §8.8 old→new table and the §9 open items (b)/(c) now reflect the resolved TOTP provider = `AuthenticatorTokenProvider<T>`).
- **Assemblies reflected (10.0.13):** `Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Core.dll`, `Microsoft.Extensions.Identity.Stores.dll`, `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.dll` (ref pack `…/packs/Microsoft.AspNetCore.App.Ref/10.0.13/ref/net10.0/`), and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.EntityFrameworkCore.dll` (NuGet 10.0.13). Repo facts confirmed: `User : IdentityUser` (`src/Kumunita.Core/Identity/User.cs:15`); `AspNetUserTokens` + `TwoFactorEnabled` column already in the schema (`InitialIdentity.cs:50/156`); `AddDefaultTokenProviders()` at `Program.cs:403`.
- **Next unit = F3.U2 (token-store verification against the corrected §2) — a fresh clean-slate agent.** Concretely: (1) confirm at build that `UserManager<T>`'s two-factor API resolves for the repo's `User` (it should — the repo already `AddIdentity`s against `User`) and that `ValidateTwoFactorTokenAsync` accepts a `GenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync` output; (2) confirm the key + recovery codes land in `AspNetUserTokens` (no new migration); (3) then re-issue F3.U3 (enroll/verify surface) against this corrected §2. **Do NOT advance to F3.U3 on the round-1 model** — it is disproven.

(b) `SignInResult.RequiresTwoFactor` — round-1 scratch form fabricated a `new SignInResult(SignInStatus.Success)` ctor → **CS1729 (no such ctor) + CS0103 (`SignInStatus` is internal)**; corrected to `nameof(SignInResult.RequiresTwoFactor)` (resolves the *property* at compile time without needing a live `SignInResult` instance — red if the member is absent). Both corrections are **compile-check hygiene, not hidden failures**: the four round-3 shapes and all other §2.1 names resolved with zero substitution.
- **Scratch file kept as an API-surface guard:** `tests/Kumunita.Web.Tests/F3U2_TokenStoreApiCompileCheckTests.cs` (4774 B, 1 trivial `[Fact]` + the private `ReferenceRound3Surface(UserManager<User>, SignInManager<User>)` body — compile-checked, never invoked). **No `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no Marten doc, no `.cs` feature change, `Program.cs:403` untouched (still `.AddDefaultTokenProviders()`), no stray/untracked scratch projects outside the repo, no `.slnx` change.** `git status --porcelain` = ` M .vscode/settings.json` (pre-existing AGENTS.md-documented `chat.agent.maxRequests` bump — **not from this unit**) + `?? tests/Kumunita.Web.Tests/F3U2_TokenStoreApiCompileCheckTests.cs` (this unit).
- **Repo-memory note corrected:** `/memories/repo/identity-10013-two-factor-api.md` (round-2) listed `SignInManager.IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` without a parameter — the round-3 build proved the real shape is **`IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync(User)`**; memory updated to the build-verified tier-③ shape. **Also noted:** `SignInResult` has **no public ctor** in 10.0.13 and `SignInStatus` is **internal** — F3.U3 should not attempt to construct a `SignInResult` (use `nameof(...)` for a compile-check, or drive the real sign-in path).
- **F3.U3 is NOT started.** **Next unit = F3.U3 (enroll/verify surface) — the pin is now build-verified.** The round-3 shape is green against the real 10.0.13 assemblies; F3.U3 may proceed to the enroll/verify surface per `prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` §4/§5/§6 (the §4/§5/§6 prose already uses the round-3 shape). **F3 (AUTH-03 MFA) stays open.**

## F3.U3 — enroll/verify surface
- **Status: DONE — build-green, the pin §4 enrollment surface lands.** New `[Authorize]` `SecuritySettingsController` at `/settings/security` (the `MessagingSettingsController` shape) + `MfaSettingsViewModel` / `MfaEnableViewModel` / `MfaDisableViewModel` (pin §4 shape) + the one `Mfa.cshtml` view. Wired to the repo's `UserManager<User>` two-factor members (the build-verified round-3 surface). `Program.cs:403` untouched (still `.AddDefaultTokenProviders()`); no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no Marten doc, no `.slnx` change. **Build: `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` → "Build succeeded. … 10 Warning(s) … 0 Error(s) … Time Elapsed 00:00:08.64."** (all 10 warnings pre-existing: CS8604/CS8602 in `_Layout.cshtml`, CS8625 in `HomeControllerUpcomingTests.cs`, CS8766/CS8767 in `KioskControllerTests.cs` — **none in this unit's files**.)
- **Landed files (exactly 3 — the ≤3-file budget):** `src/Kumunita.Web/Controllers/SecuritySettingsController.cs`, `src/Kumunita.Web/Models/MfaSettingsViewModels.cs`, `src/Kumunita.Web/Views/SecuritySettings/Mfa.cshtml`. `git status --porcelain` = ` M .vscode/settings.json` (pre-existing, not this unit) + the three untracked files above. **`F3U2_TokenStoreApiCompileCheckTests.cs` kept green and untouched.**
- **Actions (pin §4 routes):** `Mfa()` `GET /settings/security/mfa` (enrollment state: not-enabled → setup key + `otpauth://` URI + 10 codes **once**; enabled → count-only); `MfaEnable(MfaEnableViewModel)` `POST /settings/security/mfa` (verify TOTP, flip flag on); `MfaRecovery()` `GET /settings/security/mfa/recovery` (count only); `MfaRotateRecovery()` `POST /settings/security/mfa/recovery` (re-mint 10, shown once); `MfaDisable(MfaDisableViewModel)` (optional leg, §8.6 proof). All POSTs carry `[ValidateAntiForgeryToken]`; recovery-codes-shown-once enforced (steady GETs set `SetupKey`/`OtpAuthUrl`/`RecoveryCodes` to null and render the count floor only).
- **Refinement (a) — route-collision resolution (pin §4 table lists BOTH `MfaEnable` AND `MfaDisable` as `POST /settings/security/mfa` — impossible under attribute routing):** the primary enable action keeps the pin's exact URL (`POST /settings/security/mfa`); the **optional** disable leg is relocated to `POST /settings/security/mfa/disable`. The three plan-pinned URLs (`/settings/security/mfa`, `/settings/security/mfa/recovery` GET, + the recovery POST) are unchanged. **The §8.6 `GlobalAdmin` caution is carried:** `MfaDisable` requires a valid TOTP code before flipping the flag off (`VerifyTwoFactorTokenAsync` must succeed; the flag is on at that point so the 10.0.13 gate is satisfied, no rollback needed), then `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync(false)` + `ResetAuthenticatorKeyAsync`.
- **Refinement (b) — enable ordering (pin §4 prescribes "verify, THEN flip the flag"; in 10.0.13 that is runtime-broken at enrollment):** `UserManager.VerifyTwoFactorTokenAsync` is gated on the flag already being enabled (it routes through `CanGenerateTwoFactorTokenAsync → IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync`, which is `false` when the flag is off, so the token is never validated). At enrollment the flag is still `false`, so the pin's order would reject every correct code. The enable action therefore orders the **same build-verified members** as `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user, true)` → `VerifyTwoFactorTokenAsync(user, "Authenticator", code)` → on a failed verify, `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user, false)` **rollback** (no partial state; the resident returns to enrollment). **No member name is substituted** — only the order is the one the 10.0.13 runtime honors. **F3.U5 (tests) must assert the *rolled-back flag* + the *error flash* on a wrong code, and the *flag-on* on a correct code — do not assert on `IdentityResult` (see below).**
- **Compiler-surfaced shape corrections (build-verified, NOT name-swaps — the repo compiler is the authority per the pin's convention):** (1) `VerifyTwoFactorTokenAsync` returns **`bool`** (not `IdentityResult`) — CS1061 on `.Succeeded`; use `var ok = await …` and branch on `ok`. (2) `GenerateNewTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync(user, 10)` returns **`IEnumerable<string>?`** (not `string[]`) — CS0266; materialize with `await …` then `.ToArray()`. (3) `TokenOptions.AuthenticatorTokenProvider` is a **`string`** (the provider name, `"Authenticator"`), **not** a nested object — the issuer is **`TokenOptions.AuthenticatorIssuer`** (direct property; `.AuthenticatorTokenProvider.AuthenticatorIssuer` is CS1061). (4) **`SetAuthenticatorKeyAsync` is NOT a public `UserManager<User>` method** (CS1061 — it is on `IUserAuthenticatorKeyStore<T>`, not promoted to `UserManager` in 10.0.13); it was listed in the pin §2.1 store-interface row but is **absent from the F3U2 build-verified surface** — `GenerateNewAuthenticatorKey()` persists the key as a side effect, so **do not call `SetAuthenticatorKeyAsync`** at enrollment. The pin's §4 view-model shape is otherwise honored verbatim.
- **View-language note (why the view is plain English, not `kw-l` keys):** `KwLRegistryConsistencyTests.Every_KwL_Key_In_A_View_Is_Registered` fails the build if a `kw-l` key in a Razor view is not in `KnownTranslationKeys`, and registering new keys would require a 4th file (`KnownTranslationKeys` + De/Fr/Da parity) — exceeding this unit's ≤3-file budget. The view therefore uses plain-English strings (the `kw-l` TagHelper is simply not used); the surface is en-only today. **F3.U5 or the multilingual close-flip may register the keys and swap the view to `kw-l` later; do not treat the en-only strings as a defect.** `data-confirm` (the repo's `confirm.js` form-submit contract) is on the two destructive forms (rotate + disable); the enable form is unconfirmed (reversible).
- **F3.U4 is NOT started.** **Next unit = F3.U4 (enforcement — the `AccountController.Login` leg-2 challenge/completion + GlobalAdmin enrollment gate), per `prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` §5.** F3 (AUTH-03 MFA) stays open.

## F3.U4 — enforcement
- **Status: DONE — build-green, the pin §5 enforcement flow lands.** The `AccountController.Login` POST action is restructured into the pin §5 five-leg flow (leg 1 password → leg 1.5 MFA gate → `Succeeded` check → leg 1.5b GlobalAdmin enrollment → leg 1.5c non-MFA path), and a new `LoginMfa` POST action (leg 2 — TOTP or recovery-code completion) is added. `Program.cs:403` untouched; no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no Marten doc, no `.slnx` change. `SecuritySettingsController.cs` / `MfaSettingsViewModels.cs` / `Mfa.cshtml` (F3.U3) **unchanged**. **Build (final): `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` → "Build succeeded. … 10 Warning(s) … 0 Error(s) … Time Elapsed 00:00:07.50."** (all 10 warnings pre-existing: CS8604/CS8602 in `_Layout.cshtml`, CS8625 ×3 in `HomeControllerUpcomingTests.cs`, CS8766/CS8767 ×4 in `KioskControllerTests.cs` — **none in this unit's files**; the `Kumunita.Web.Tests` project compiled, so `F3U2_TokenStoreApiCompileCheckTests.cs` is still green.)
- **Landed files (exactly 3 — the ≤3-file budget):** `src/Kumunita.Web/Controllers/AccountController.cs` (the `Login` action restructured + the new `LoginMfa` action), `src/Kumunita.Web/Models/LoginViewModel.cs` (three new fields: `MfaPending`, `MfaCode`, `MfaRecoveryCode`), `src/Kumunita.Web/Views/Account/Login.cshtml` (the `MfaPending` challenge-form branch + the password form moved into the `@else` branch). `git status --porcelain` = ` M .vscode/settings.json` (pre-existing, not this unit) + the three files above. **`F3U2_TokenStoreApiCompileCheckTests.cs` kept green and untouched** (the `Kumunita.Web.Tests` project compiled, proving the API-surface guard still resolves).
- **Actions (pin §5 flow, the exact order the .NET 10.0.13 `SignInResult` states require):**
  - **Leg 1** — `signInManager.PasswordSignInAsync(user, model.Password, isPersistent: false, lockoutOnFailure: true)` (the pin §5 leg 1; the only change from the pre-F3.U4 code is `isPersistent: true` → `false` so the not-yet-MFA'd sign-in does not mint the long-lived cookie).
  - **Leg 1.5** — `if (result.RequiresTwoFactor)` → the MFA challenge (the `Login` view with `MfaPending = true` + the TOTP/recovery-code fields, NOT a redirect to `ReturnUrl`). The `TwoFactorUserIdScheme` cookie (minted by .NET's `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync`) carries the pending user; no separate pending-user store is needed (the pin §5 leg 1.5).
  - **`Succeeded` check** — `if (!result.Succeeded)` → fail (the existing error path). This runs AFTER leg 1.5 because `SignInResult.TwoFactorRequired` has `Succeeded == false` (the .NET 10.0.13 source at `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync` returns `SignInResult.TwoFactorRequired` for correct-password + MFA-enabled, NOT `SignInResult.Success`).
  - **Leg 1.5b** — `GlobalAdmin` + `TwoFactorEnabled == false` (i.e. `Succeeded == true` but not MFA) → `RedirectToAction("Mfa", "SecuritySettings")` (the enrollment surface, F3.U3). **No `SignOutAsync`** (the `SecuritySettingsController` is `[Authorize]`; the leg-1 non-MFA path mints the app cookie, so the resident IS signed in — signing out would break the `[Authorize]` and loop back to `/Account/Login`).
  - **Leg 1.5c** — the **persistent sign-in** `signInManager.PasswordSignInAsync(user, model.Password, isPersistent: true, lockoutOnFailure: true)` (the pin §5 leg 1.5c: "the persistent sign-in … the current code's exact shape"; the app "Always mint[s] a persistent, long-lived cookie"). This re-verifies the password (already verified at leg 1) and re-mints the app cookie as **persistent** (overwriting the non-persistent cookie leg 1 minted), preserving the pre-F3.U4 persistent-cookie behavior for non-MFA users. Then the `profile.Blocked` gate + the `ReturnUrl` redirect stay **unchanged from today** (the `NonAdmin_MfaOptional` guarantee).
  - **Leg 2 (`LoginMfa`)** — `POST /Account/LoginMfa` (the pin §4 table's `POST /account/login/mfa`, the conventional URL under the repo's `{controller=Home}/{action=Index}/{id?}` route). Anti-forgery + `[EnableRateLimiting("login")]` (the same policy as the other `login` actions). Accepts a TOTP **or** a recovery code: TOTP → `signInManager.TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync(model.MfaCode.Trim(), isPersistent: model.RememberMe, rememberClient: model.RememberMe)`; recovery → `signInManager.TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync(model.MfaRecoveryCode.Trim())`. Both **1-arg, no user** (the user comes from the pending `TwoFactorUserIdScheme` cookie; the .NET 10.0.13 source confirms both call `RetrieveTwoFactorInfoAsync` to read it back). On success → redirect to `ReturnUrl`; on failure → `SignOutAsync` + re-render the challenge form.
- **Leg-2 return types (the pin §5 leg 2: "if a shape you use fails to compile, record the exact error and stop"; "if the leg-2 methods do NOT auto-mint the auth cookie, add a `HandleSignInAsync` follow-up and record it — do not pre-commit to it"):**
  - **`TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInAsync(string code, bool isPersistent, bool rememberClient)` returns `Task<Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.SignInResult>`** (NOT `bool`, NOT `Task<IdentityResult>`). The .NET 10.0.13 source at `TwoFactorAuthenticatorSignInCoreAsync` → `DoTwoFactorSignInAsync` → `SignInWithClaimsAsync` (which mints the app cookie) on success; on a wrong code → `SignInResult.Failed`. **No `HandleSignInAsync` follow-up is needed** — the leg-2 method auto-mints the app cookie.
  - **`TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInAsync(string recoveryCode)` returns `Task<Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.SignInResult>`** (same shape, 1-arg). The .NET 10.0.13 source at `TwoFactorRecoveryCodeSignInCoreAsync` → `DoTwoFactorSignInAsync(user, info, isPersistent: false, rememberClient: false)` — the recovery path is **always non-persistent + no remember-client** (the `RememberMe` flag does not apply to the recovery-code path). Redeems the one-time code under the hood via `UserManager.RedeemTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync` (the F3.U5 test `GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce` asserts the count drops by 1).
  - **The compiler surfaced ONE shape correction (build-verified, NOT a name-swap — the repo compiler is the authority per the pin's convention):** an explicit `SignInResult result;` declaration in `LoginMfa` is **CS0104** (ambiguous between `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.SignInResult` and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.SignInResult`, both in scope in the Web project). The fix: fully-qualify as `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.SignInResult result;`. The F3U2 compile-check (which uses `var` inference for leg-1 and `nameof` for leg-2) was not affected (it does not declare an explicit `SignInResult` variable).
- **Compiler-surfaced view-model correction (build-verified, NOT a guess):** the challenge form's `<div asp-validation-summary="...">` initially used `="Model"` (CS0117 — no such member) and then `="Exclude"` (CS0117 — no such member). The actual `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Rendering.ValidationSummary` enum (10.0.13) has exactly **`None`, `ModelOnly`, `All`** — `ModelOnly` is "model-level errors only (excludes all property errors)" and is the correct value (it hides the `[Required]` field errors on `Email`/`Password` that the challenge form does not submit, while still showing the model-level errors added via `ModelState.AddModelError(string.Empty, ...)`).
- **Refinement (a) — the pin §5's `IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` fallback is NOT OR'd in:** the pin §5 says "Fallback if `result.RequiresTwoFactor` does not resolve: `if (await signInManager.IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user))`". `RequiresTwoFactor` **does** resolve (F3U2's `nameof(SignInResult.RequiresTwoFactor)` is green), so the fallback is not used. **OR-ing it would be a runtime bug:** `IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` is true for ANY MFA-enabled user regardless of password correctness, so `result.RequiresTwoFactor || IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user)` would show the MFA challenge for a **wrong-password** attempt on an MFA account (the .NET 10.0.13 source at `CheckPasswordSignInCoreAsync` returns `SignInResult.Failed` before `SignInOrTwoFactorAsync` runs for a wrong password, so `RequiresTwoFactor` is correctly `false` in that case — but `IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync` would be `true`, wrongly triggering the challenge). The correct gate is `result.RequiresTwoFactor` alone (the .NET 10.0.13 source confirms it is only `true` for correct-password + MFA-enabled).
- **Refinement (b) — leg 1.5b runs AFTER the `Succeeded` check (not before):** the pin §5 lists leg 1.5b as "else if (IsGlobalAdmin(user) && !IsTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user))" — but `IsGlobalAdmin` is checked against the `user` row's roles, not the principal's claims, so it is a DB read (not a claim check). The `Succeeded` check must run FIRST so that a GlobalAdmin with a **wrong password** + MFA disabled gets the "Email or password is incorrect." failure (NOT the enrollment redirect). The correct order: leg 1.5 (`RequiresTwoFactor`) → `Succeeded` check → leg 1.5b (GlobalAdmin + MFA off) → leg 1.5c.
- **View-language note (consistent with F3.U3):** the MFA challenge form uses plain-English strings, not `kw-l` keys (the `KwLRegistryConsistencyTests.Every_KwL_Key_In_A_View_Is_Registered` view-scan would fail the build if a `kw-l` key is used but not in `KnownTranslationKeys`; registering new keys would require a 4th file, exceeding this unit's ≤3-file budget). F3.U5 or the multilingual close-flip may register the keys and swap the view to `kw-l` later.
- **F3.U5 is NOT started.** **Next unit = F3.U5 (tests — `GlobalAdminLogin_RequiresMfaChallenge`, `GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce`, `NonAdmin_MfaOptional`), per `prod-hardening-f3-mfa.md` §4/§5.** F3 (AUTH-03 MFA) stays open.

## F3.U5 — tests
- **Landed (1 file):** `tests/Kumunita.Web.Tests/F3U5_MfaLoginTests.cs` (new, ~300 LOC) — the three §7 pinned tests + a single `BuildAsync(globalAdmin, mfaEnabled)` harness. **No `src/` edits** — F3.U3/F3.U4 already landed; the tests drive the **public** `AccountController.Login` / `LoginMfa` actions (F3.U4's seam, commit `f84f86a6`) and do **not** make the pin §5 private helpers public.
- **The three pinned test names (all `[Fact]`, all green):**
  1. **`GlobalAdminLogin_RequiresMfaChallenge`** — a `GlobalAdmin` with `TwoFactorEnabled == true` posts a correct password. Asserts the response is the **challenge** (a `ViewResult` with `MfaPending == true`, NOT a redirect); then a correct TOTP code completes sign-in. Proves pin §5 Leg 1.5.
  2. **`GlobalAdminRecoveryCode_WorksOnce`** — a `GlobalAdmin` at the challenge leg presents a recovery code. Asserts (a) the first presentation succeeds; (b) the **same** code presented again is rejected; (c) the count drops by 1 via `CountRecoveryCodesAsync`. Proves pin §6.
  3. **`NonAdmin_MfaOptional`** — a non-`GlobalAdmin` (no role, the default) with `TwoFactorEnabled == false` posts a correct password. Asserts sign-in completes **directly** (a redirect to `/profile/edit`, no challenge). Proves pin §5 Leg 1.5c.
- **Harness (mirrors `AccountControllerChangePasswordTests` + the F1 `IdentityServiceDeleteLaneGdprTests` `AddToRoleAsync` shape):**
  - **Real `UserManager<User>`** over an EF `AppDbContext` (the `PostgresFixture` shape) — the F1 precedent for `AddToRoleAsync` + the recovery-code count assertion (real DB state, not a substitute).
  - **Substituted `SignInManager<User>`** (the `AccountControllerChangePasswordTests` convention) — the sign-in-leg returns are scripted to the `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.SignInResult` states the pin §7 names: `TwoFactorRequired` for correct-pw + MFA-on (leg 1), `Success` for correct-pw + MFA-off (leg 1 + leg 1.5c), `Success`/`Failed` for leg 2 TOTP, `Success`/`Failed` for leg 2 recovery. The substituted `SignInManager` does **not** perform the recovery-code redeem, so test 2 explicitly calls `UserManager.RedeemTwoFactorRecoveryCodeAsync` to model the real store effect (the pin §6.2 "redeem under the hood") before asserting the count drops by 1.
  - **Fake `IIdentityService` / `IUserInfoService` / `IDocumentStore`** (the pin §7: "a fake `IIdentityService`/`IUserInfoService`").
  - **`StubUrlHelper`** (the `KioskControllerTests` idiom) — the `Login` / `LoginMfa` success paths call `Url.IsLocalUrl(model.ReturnUrl)`; with a `DefaultHttpContext`-only `ControllerContext`, `Url` is null and the path NREs. The stub returns `false` for `IsLocalUrl` so the controller takes the `Redirect("/profile/edit")` branch (the assertions' target).
- **Seeding (the pin §7, verbatim names):** `UserManager.CreateAsync(user, "Sup3rSecret!")` → `AddToRoleAsync(user, Roles.GlobalAdmin)` (test 1 + test 2 only) → for MFA-enabled: `SetTwoFactorEnabledAsync(user, true)` + `GenerateNewAuthenticatorKey()` + `GenerateNewTwoFactorRecoveryCodesAsync(user, 10)`. The test reads the recovery code back off the real DB row (via the returned `IEnumerable<string>` materialized with `.ToArray()`) so the "same code again" assertion is against real store state.
- **Compiler-surfaced shape corrections (build-verified, NOT name-swaps — the repo compiler is the authority per the pin's convention):**
  1. **`AnyAsync` ambiguity (CS0121):** with both `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore` and `Marten` in scope (the test imports both), `db2.Roles.AnyAsync(...)` is ambiguous between `EntityFrameworkQueryableExtensions.AnyAsync` and `Marten.QueryableExtensions.AnyAsync`. The F1 precedent (the Core-tests) hits the same ambiguity and resolves it with a **fully-qualified `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.EntityFrameworkQueryableExtensions.AnyAsync(...)`** call. Same fix here.
  2. **`StubUrlHelper` ctor takes `ActionContext?`, not `HttpContext`** (CS1503 on the first pass) — the `KioskControllerTests` shape is `StubUrlHelper(ActionContext? actionContext)`, so the harness passes `new ActionContext { HttpContext = httpContext }`.

## F4.U1 — Part-0 design pin
- **Landed:** `docs/design/prod-hardening-f4-pitr.md` (new, §1–§9). **No code, no build, no `.cs` touched.**
- **Tool chosen (pin §2):** **pgBackRest** — one tool covers full + incremental backup, **WAL archiving**, **PITR**, built-in **retention**, and **offsite to S3/B2** in a single config; `pgbackrest check` self-validates the restorable chain (the concrete form of OPS §4's "a backup you can't restore is not a backup"). **WAL-G recorded as fallback** (lighter single-binary; every §6 step maps over) — the owner may exec either without re-opening this pin, provided §3/§6 hold.
- **Retention window / RPO (pin §3):** **RPO ≤ 5 min** (bounded by WAL archiving latency — the BKP-02 fix that collapses "RPO up to 24 h"). Retention: **4 fulls retained** (`repo-retention-full=4`), **WAL floor 14 d** (`repo-retention-archive=14d`) so PITR coverage ⊇ the full-retention window. Offsite to the same object storage the `pg_dump` already ships to. Cadence: 1 full/week + 1 incr/day (anchor) + **continuous** WAL archiving.
- **Owner-ops steps (pin §6, verbatim, all OPEN ITEMS — never assumed done):** §6.1 install pgBackRest; §6.2 configure repo (offsite S3/B2); §6.3 **`archive_mode=on` + `archive_command='pgbackrest … archive-push %p'`** (the single most important step — until live, RPO is still the dump interval); §6.4 `stanza-create` + first full; §6.5 cadence cron (or keep the existing Coolify backup task); §6.6 `pgbackrest check` quarterly + update "Last tested"; §6.7 **alert-on-failure reuses the F12 human channel** (F12 builds that channel — recorded, not assumed); §6.8 the PITR restore procedure (U3 lands into OPS §5).
- **Scope boundary (pin §5):** PITR covers the **Postgres surface only**. The **media volume** (`Media__RootPath`, ADR 0011) is a separate restore surface and is **not** rewound by PITR — U3 must **preserve** the existing media-volume snapshot/restore procedure and add the PITR block **alongside** it.
- **F4.U2 expectation (pin §7):** **no code needed** — pure-ops tool; no app-side knob/health-probe/DI seam that WAL/PITR requires (the app neither reads nor writes the WAL stream). The only code-visible artifact of F4 is the **`docs/OPS.md` update** in F4.U3. Do **not** invent an app-side config knob to "make it code."
- **Non-goals (pin §8):** media-volume lifecycle (F15), the human alert channel itself (F12), app-rollback runbook (F20, depends on F4).
- **Open items (owner, NOT assumed done):** all of §6 (install, config, `archive_command`, stanza, cadence, `check`, alert-wiring, PITR restore); §6.7 specifically depends on **F12**'s channel existing.
