The pin grounds the F17 gate at the **actual `StageAsync` call site (line 361)** and the **actual ADR 0078 code block (line ~238)** — both verified by read, not the plan's line-number shorthand. No other seam invented.
- **Open items (owner, **recorded, NOT assumed done**):** **(1)** point the production mail provider's bounce/complaint webhook at `POST https://<instance>/api/mail/suppress` with the shared-secret header set (the `MailSuppression__IngestToken` value) + confirm a test bounce arrives — **until this runs, the suppression list is empty and the gate is a no-op** (the active control is the existing per-recipient opt-out + the platform's `nosniff` posture). **(2)** optional: extend the gate to the other `StageAsync` call sites (`IdentityService.cs:182/244` verification emails, `EventReminderService.cs:221` reminders, `NotificationFlushService.cs:170` deferred-email flush) — the pin gates the **primary** per-recipient path only; the others are lower-volume / known-good recipients. **(3)** optional: a manual suppression admin lane (the `Reason="manual"`/`Source="manual"` fields are pinned for it; the UI is out of scope for F17).
- **F17.U1 is the FIRST unit of F17 (U1–U3 of F17).** F17.U2 (the suppression doc + gate) and F17.U3 (the `ISuppressionService` + Web ingest route + the `SuppressedAddress_NotMailed` pinned test) remain, then F18–F22. A nested `public sealed class SuppressionIngestRequest` (the `public` is required — a public action's parameter type must not be less accessible, CS0051). Ctor takes `ISuppressionService` + `IConfiguration`.
  5. **`tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/F17U3_SuppressionGateTests.cs` (NEW)** — the pinned test **`SuppressedAddress_NotMailed`** (pin §4.1, **UNRENAMED** — the next audit run diffs against it) + the pin §4.2 three-leg harness: (1) `Assert.Equal(0, mailer.StageAsyncCount)` (the email is NOT staged — the gate fired before `StageAsync`); (2) the inbox row IS stored (query by `IdempotencyKey`); (3) no `AccessAudit` row written (the gate is a read-only lookup). A `SuppressedAddress` row is planted for the recipient's email.

## F18.U1 — Part-0 design pin (FIRST UNIT OF F18)
- **Landed (exactly ONE new doc file — `docs/design/prod-hardening-f18-mailalarms.md`; **no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test**):** the Part-0 design pin for F18 (MAIL-06, `code+ops`, dep F12) — §1 the metrics source, §2 the thresholds (T1–T4 with pinned values), §3 the F12 alert channel the alarm fires into, §4 the pinned owner-ops steps (verbatim provider checklist), §5 the invariants, §6 the U2/U3 split, §7 the open items, §8 this handoff contract.
- **The five pin targets (grounded in the real repo, not invented):**
  1. **§1 Metrics source** — PRIMARY (owner-ops, U3): the production mail provider's own bounce/complaint/volume console/API (the provider is owner-chosen per OPS §7 — Resend/Postmark/SES/Mailgun; the pin gives the per-provider console branch for each alarm). SECONDARY (in-repo, U2's cross-check leg): the **F17 `SuppressedAddress`** list (landed, `src/Kumunita.Core/Notifications/SuppressedAddress.cs`, on `NotificationDocTypes` — the `Created` + `Reason` fields give the in-repo rate) + the existing **`EmailDeadLetter`** count (`mt.email_dead_letters`, read-only context; the `/health` gate is **untouched**). **No new sent-volume ledger** (T1–T3's denominator — "unique recipients sent" — is the provider's own data; inventing an in-app ledger would be scope creep past a hardening fix).
  2. **§2 Thresholds (concrete values, U2 enforces T4, U3 configures T1–T3 at the provider):** T1 bounce-rate **≥ 2 % / 24 h** (alert) + T1′ **≥ 5 % / 24 h** (critical — the provider-rejection line); T2 complaint-rate **≥ 0.1 % / 7 d OR ≥ 1 complaint** (whichever first — at T1 volume one complaint IS the signal); T3 volume spike **any 1-h window ≥ 3× the median 1-h volume of the preceding 7 days**; T4 (the ONLY in-repo leg, U2) **≥ 5 new `SuppressedAddress` rows** (`Reason` ∈ bounce/complaint) **in 24 h**. Rationale recorded: the 2 %/0.1 %/5 % lines are the de-facto transactional-mail deliverability thresholds (Gmail bulk-sender guidelines, provider suppression defaults).
  3. **§3 The F12 channel (reused, not reinvented):** the new kind is **`"mail.bounce-alarm"`** — the exact string the F11 pin pre-reserved (`AlertEvent` `Kind`-as-`string` comment: "F18 adds `mail.bounce-alarm`, F12 routes regardless of kind"). Payload pinned: `Subject` = alarm name + breached threshold, `Detail` = `Reason` breakdown (bounce: N, complaint: M — no emails in the body, they stay in the DB), `Attempts` = **null** (the F11 pin's "non-retry alert" case). U2 calls the **existing** `IAlertSink.ReportAsync` (F12's `SupportEmailAlertSink` wins DI in production; `NoopAlertSink` floor kept; best-effort-at-the-boundary `try/catch` swallow). **No new sink, no new method, no F11/F12/F10 file change.** Provider-side T1–T3 do NOT flow through `IAlertSink` (the provider's own notification path) but deliver to the **same** monitored mailbox (`Community__SupportEmail`) — one shared destination, two independent delivery paths (the `code+ops` split made explicit).
  4. **§4 Owner-ops steps (U3 verbatim, 7 steps):** record provider + alarm IDs per instance (the "last verified" idiom, F4/F20); create the bounce rule (2 % / 5 % per provider console); the complaint rule (0.1 % / 7 d or first complaint); the volume rule (3× median, or record explicitly that the provider has no native rule and T4 is the functional substitute — do not fake a rule that doesn't exist); **destination confirmation = F12's open item (a) re-stated as a precondition** (one unmonitored inbox defeats all three alarms); cross-check drill (test bounce → provider console shows it → `SuppressedAddress` row appears → if ≥ 5 in 24 h, the F12-channel alert arrives); stamp "last verified". Precondition: **F17's webhook open item must be closed first** (without it the `SuppressedAddress` list is empty and T4 never fires).
  5. **§5 Invariants:** design-only U1 (one doc); one channel not two (reuse F12); one data source not a new one (read the F17 list + `EmailDeadLetter`, **no new doc type / no new surface / no sent-volume ledger**); Core stays HTTP-free (ADR 0006-D — U2's tick is a Core Wolverine recurring handler, the F15/JOB-04 advisory-lock idiom); the `/health` degraded gate UNCHANGED (F18 is additive, like F11/F12); best-effort-at-the-boundary; owner-ops recorded-not-assumed; no PII in the alert body beyond what F17 already stores.
- **Open items (owner, **recorded, NOT assumed done**):** (1) all of §4 — T1/T1′/T2/T3 provider rules created + active, destination confirmed monitored, drill run, "last verified" stamped; **until then T1–T3 do not exist** (provider-side) and the only alarm is T4 (U2's leg); (2) F12 pin §7(a) — confirm prod `Community__SupportEmail` is a real, monitored mailbox (the shared destination of both legs); (3) F17 open item (precondition) — the provider webhook pointed at `POST /api/mail/suppress` with the `X-Suppression-Token` header (the F17 pin §7, carried).
- **Invariants held (this unit):** design-only (one doc file, nothing else touched); grounded in the landed F12 channel (`SupportEmailAlertSink`, the F11 pin's pre-reserved kind string) + the landed F17 `SuppressedAddress` seam (grep-verified in `docs/design/prod-hardening-f17-bounce.md` §1–§3 + the F17.U2/U3 handoff entries) — no invented seams; owner-ops recorded-not-assumed (the F17.U3/F16.U3/F10.U3/F12.U3 pattern); the F12 channel is reused, a second sink is NOT designed.
- **Drift pause:** **none** — the F11 pin's `AlertEvent`/`IAlertSink` contract (`docs/design/prod-hardening-f11-dlq-alert.md` §2b), the F12 pin's channel + open items, and the F17 pin's `SuppressedAddress` shape all matched the entry reads exactly. OPS §7's provider-agnostic phrasing ("Choose one and record it per instance") was honored by pinning per-provider branches rather than assuming one provider.
- **F18 is now at U2 (F18.U1 COMPLETE — the pin).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F18.U2–U3 + F19–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F18.U2** (the `MailAlarmsTick` code leg, the T4 threshold + the `"mail.bounce-alarm"` `AlertEvent`) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads this entry + the pin §6 (the U2 shape) + §2 T4 (the threshold) + §3 (the payload mapping + channel) + §5 (the invariants).

## F18.U2 — the `MailAlarmsTick` code leg (SECOND UNIT OF F18)
- **Landed (4 touchpoints — 2 `Kumunita.Core` files + 2 Web glue, the F15 `MediaLifecycleTick` house shape verbatim; **no `<PackageReference>`, no new doc type, no new doc-surface registration, no migration, no `.slnx` change**):**
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Notifications/MailAlarmsTick.cs` — **NEW**, `public sealed record MailAlarmsTick() : Wolverine.TimeoutMessage(TimeSpan.FromHours(1));` (the `UsagePurgeTick`/`MediaLifecycleTick` shape verbatim — **1-hour** delay baked in, the pin §2 T4 cadence; no per-callsite `DelayedFor`).
  - `src/Kumunita.Core/Notifications/MailAlarmsService.cs` — **NEW**, **static** Wolverine-free (HTTP-free, ADR 0006-D) service: `public const int AlertThreshold = 5;` + `public const int WindowHours = 24;` + `public const string AlertKind = "mail.bounce-alarm";` (pin §2 T4 + §3) + `public static async Task<bool> EvaluateAsync(IDocumentStore, IAlertSink?, DateTimeOffset now, CancellationToken)`.
  - `src/Kumunita.Web/SideEffects/MailAlarmsHandler.cs` — **NEW**, thin adapter mirroring `MediaLifecycleHandler`: `Task<IEnumerable<object>> Handle(MailAlarmsTick, IDocumentStore, IAlertSink)` → `await MailAlarmsService.EvaluateAsync(store, alertSink, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow);` then `return new[] { new MailAlarmsTick() };` (returns the array, not `yield return` — the async-eligible cascade shape).
  - `src/Kumunita.Web/Program.cs` — **one `using Kumunita.Core.Notifications;`** (line 7) + **one seed line** in the existing "Kick off the recurring §6.4 jobs … on boot" block, beside the `MediaLifecycleTick`/`UsagePurgeTick` seeds: `await bus.PublishAsync(new MailAlarmsTick());` (with the F18/MAIL-06 T4 explanatory comment).
- **The T4 gate as implemented (pin §2 T4 / §6, verbatim):** in ONE **read-only** `IDocumentStore.QuerySession()` (a `QuerySession` is read-only by construction — the "no write" invariant is enforced by the type; zero `SaveChangesAsync`), count `SuppressedAddress` rows with `Created >= now.AddHours(-24)` AND `Reason == "bounce"` → `bounces`; the same with `Reason == "complaint"` → `complaints`; and `EmailDeadLetter` total → `deadLetters`. **`total = bounces + complaints`; if `total < 5` return `false` (no alert).** The `Reason` filter is the pin §2 T4 closed set — `"manual"` rows do NOT trip the alarm (the gate is the provider bounce/complaint ingest, not manual suppression). The `EmailDeadLetter` count is the pin §1 second signal, read **into `Detail` only** — the `/health` degraded gate is **UNCHANGED** (pin §5.5; the `HealthController` / `IEmailDeadLetterCounter` path is untouched).
- **The alert payload as implemented (pin §3, verbatim — no PII, pin §5.8):** `new AlertEvent(Kind: "mail.bounce-alarm", Subject: $"SuppressedAddress ingest: {total} new bounce/complaint rows in 24h (threshold 5)", Detail: $"bounce: {bounces}, complaint: {complaints}; EmailDeadLetter count: {deadLetters}", Attempts: null)`. Counts + the `Reason` breakdown only — **no suppressed e-mails in the body** (they stay in the DB). `Attempts = null` (the F11 pin's "non-retry alert" case). The `Kind` is the F11 pin's pre-reserved exact string (`AlertSink.cs` `AlertEvent` doc: "F18 adds `mail.bounce-alarm`, F12 routes regardless of kind").
- **The channel as implemented (pin §3 — F12's, reused, NOT reinvented):** `EvaluateAsync` fires the alert through the **existing** `IAlertSink.ReportAsync(alert, ct)` (the Core `Kumunita.Core.Identity` seam). **Best-effort at the boundary (pin §5.6 / the `IAlertSink` contract):** the call is wrapped in `try/catch` swallow — a channel failure never propagates (the `SuppressedAddress` rows + the provider console are the load-bearing signals, the alert is a copy). A `null` `alertSink` (a harness-construction site) is a **no-op** (the F11 `OutboxEmailHandler.HandleFault` `if (alertSink is not null)` idiom) — the `NoopAlertSink` floor is unchanged. In production the DI binding (the F12 `Program.cs` gate: `Community:SupportEmail` non-empty → `SupportEmailAlertSink`, else `NoopAlertSink`) resolves the channel; **no F11/F12/F10 file was touched**, no new sink, no new method on the seam.
- **Cadence / idempotency as implemented (pin §6, the F15 house shape):** self-rescheduling `Wolverine.TimeoutMessage(1 hour)` + static Wolverine-free service + thin Web handler + boot seed. **Idempotency = natural no-op** (a read-only count — a double-run just re-counts the same window and re-fires at most once per breach window; pin §6 endorses the "still broken, still loud" re-fire semantics — one email per hour while breached, not a storm). **No Postgres advisory lock** — see drift below.
- **Invariants held (pin §5):** Core stays HTTP-free (`MailAlarmsService` is static, store-injected, no Web types / `IHttpContextAccessor`); one channel not two (F12's `IAlertSink` reused); one data source not a new one (the F17 `SuppressedAddress` list + the existing `EmailDeadLetter` — **no new doc type / no new surface / no sent-volume ledger**); the `/health` degraded gate UNCHANGED (the tick only *reads* `EmailDeadLetter`); best-effort-at-the-boundary (`try/catch` swallow); **no PII in the alert body** (counts + `Reason` breakdown only); owner-ops (T1–T3) **NOT** in this unit (that is F18.U3, pin §4).
- **Build result:** `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` — **Build succeeded, 0 Error(s), 52 Warning(s), 30.73 s elapsed** (the 52 warnings are pre-existing repo-wide build warnings — **none reference the new `MailAlarms*` files**, confirmed by grep of the build log). The sync terminal channel wedged twice (the known Copilot agent terminal bug — `microsoft/vscode#286106` / `#265881` / `#265863`, AGENTS.md "Running terminal commands safely"); the result was captured by redirecting to `.tmp/f18u2-build.log` and reading it with the file tools. **No test run in this unit** (a test is optional per the spec — the F15 tick's test idiom, `Lifecycle_DeletesExpiredBytes`, is the shape one would mirror; F18's cross-check is read-only so a test would pin `EvaluateAsync`'s gate/payload against a stub store + a fake `IAlertSink`).
- **Drift pause (recorded, NOT guessed):** the pin §6 (and the F15/JOB-04 spec it defers to) names a "Postgres advisory lock" as the idempotency precedent. **There is NO advisory lock anywhere in `src/`** (full-`src` grep `pg_advisory`/`AdvisoryLock`/`lock_id` → 0 hits) and **none of the six existing ticks** (`AuditPurge`/`UsagePurge`/`StorageMetricsCapture`/`MediaLifecycle`/`EventReminder`/`NotificationFlush`) takes one — the **merged F15.U2 handoff entry** (the precedent this unit mirrors) explicitly reconciled this and added **no lock** (F15 pin §8.1). F18.U2 is **read-only** (even more trivially idempotent than F15's write-then-delete sweep — a double-run is a pure re-count), so the F15 resolution applies verbatim: **no advisory lock, natural-no-op idempotency + the durable `TimeoutMessage`**. Adding one would be a **new frozen seam + a lock-table migration**, beyond this unit's scope (unit-series rule 2) — and would contradict the pin's own §5.3 "no new doc-surface registration." The one **shape** divergence from the literal task wording: the spec named the registration target as "`DependencyInjection.cs` **or the F15 tick's registration seam**" — the parenthetical defers to the F15 idiom, whose **merged handoff entry** used the `Program.cs` boot seed + a Web `SideEffects` handler (NOT `DependencyInjection.cs`, which registers *domain services*, never ticks). This unit follows the F15 idiom (the parenthetical's stated deference). The "≤ 2 files" is honored as the merged F15.U2 interpreted it: **≤ 2 `Kumunita.Core` files + Web glue** (the Web handler + seed are unavoidable for Wolverine discovery, which scans the Web assembly — confirmed by all six existing ticks living in `Kumunita.Web/SideEffects/`).
- **Open items (owner, **recorded, NOT assumed done**):** **F18.U3** = the owner-ops leg (pin §4 verbatim, steps 1–7) — the T1/T1′/T2/T3 provider alarm rules created + active, the destination confirmed a real monitored mailbox (F12 pin §7(a), load-bearing here), the cross-check drill run, "last verified" stamped. **Precondition (F17's open item, carried):** the provider bounce/complaint webhook pointed at `POST /api/mail/suppress` with the `X-Suppression-Token` header — without it the `SuppressedAddress` list is empty and **T4 (this unit's leg) never fires**. Until the owner runs U3, T1–T3 do not exist (provider-side) and the only active alarm is T4.
- **F18 is now at U3 (F18.U2 COMPLETE — the `MailAlarmsTick` code leg landed, build green).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F18.U3 + F19–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F18.U3** (the owner-ops leg — pin §4 verbatim, recorded as an open item, never assumed done).

## F18.U3 — the owner-ops leg (THIRD AND FINAL UNIT OF F18)
- **Landed (exactly ONE modified file — `hardening-handoff-notes.md`, this section appended; **no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run**):** the pin §4 owner-ops steps 1–7 recorded **verbatim** below as **open items (recorded, NOT assumed done)**, the pin §7 open items re-stated, F18 marked **code-complete** (U1 pin + U2 code leg landed; U3 = owner-ops, this unit), and the forward pointer to F19. The owner executes the §4 steps against the production provider console + monitored mailbox; the agent does not.

- **The pin §4 owner-ops steps 1–7 (verbatim, **recorded, NOT assumed done**):**

  > **Precondition (F17's open item must be closed first — it is the data source's flow):** the provider's bounce/complaint webhook is pointed at `POST https://<instance>/api/mail/suppress` with the `X-Suppression-Token` header set, and at least one test bounce has produced a `SuppressedAddress` row. (F17.U3 handoff note — the F18 alarm has nothing to cross-check against until this flows.)

  1. **Record the provider + alarm IDs per instance** (OPS §7's "Choose one and record it per instance"): add to the instance's OPS record — provider name, sending domain, the alarm/notification rule IDs created in steps 2–4. (One line in the per-instance record; this is where the "last verified" date lives, per the F4/F20 runbook idiom.)
  2. **Bounce alarm (T1/T1′):** in the provider console (SES: *Notifications* → bounce notifications + *Event destinations* → SNS topic with an email subscription; Postmark: *Suppression* + *Server stats* alerts; Resend: *Alerts* → bounce-rate rule; Mailgun: *Sending → Suppressions* + *Routes/notifications*), create a rule: **bounce rate ≥ 2 % over 24 h → email notification to `<Community__SupportEmail>`**, and a **critical** rule at **≥ 5 % / 24 h** to the same address. Confirm the rule is *active* (not draft) and note its ID in step 1's record.
  3. **Complaint alarm (T2):** create a rule: **complaint rate ≥ 0.1 % over 7 d, or first complaint of any rolling 7 d → email notification to `<Community__SupportEmail>`**. (SES: complaint event on the same destination; Postmark: *Suppression* complaint alert; Resend: *Alerts* → complaint rule; Mailgun: *Suppressions* notifications.)
  4. **Volume alarm (T3):** enable the provider's daily/hourly volume anomaly or daily-digest notification (SES: *Event destinations* on `Send`/`Reject` counts or the console's *Usage* dashboard + a Uptime-Kuma-style poll per OPS §8; Postmark: *Usage* alerts; Resend: *Alerts* → volume rule; Mailgun: *Logs* digest) set to **notify `<Community__SupportEmail>` when any 1-h window is ≥ 3× the 7-day median 1-h volume**. Where the provider has no native "3× median" rule (common), the **in-repo T4 leg is the functional substitute** — record that explicitly in step 1's record rather than faking a provider-side rule that doesn't exist.
  5. **Destination confirmation (F12's open item (a), re-stated as a precondition here):** confirm the prod `Community__SupportEmail` is a **real, monitored** mailbox a human reads — steps 2–4 deliver to it, and U2's T4 alert does too. One unmonitored inbox silently defeats all three alarms. (F12 pin §7(a) — the same open item, now load-bearing for F18.)
  6. **Cross-check drill:** send one test email to a known-bouncing address (or trigger the provider's test-bounce tool); confirm (a) the provider console shows the bounce, (b) a `SuppressedAddress` row appears in the app's data (via `POST /api/mail/suppress`), (c) if ≥ 5 such rows exist in 24 h, the F12-channel alert email arrived at the monitored inbox (U2's T4 leg — only testable once U2 has landed).
  7. **Stamp "last verified"** in step 1's per-instance OPS record (the F4/F20 runbook "last verified / last drilled" idiom).

  **All of the above is recorded as an open item in this handoff note, NOT assumed done** (the F17.U3 "point the webhook" / F16.U3 "run ClamAV" / F10.U3 "set `SENTRY_DSN`" / F12.U3 "confirm a real recipient" pattern). Until the owner runs it, the only active leg is **T4** (U2's in-repo tick, fired when `SuppressedAddress` flow exists) — **T1–T3 do not exist** until configured at the provider.

- **The pin §7 open items (re-stated, owner, **recorded, NOT assumed done**):**
  1. **All of §4** (steps 1–7 above) — provider alarm rules T1/T1′/T2/T3 created and active, destination confirmed monitored, cross-check drill run, "last verified" stamped. Until then, **T1–T3 do not exist** (provider-side) and the only alarm is **T4** (U2's in-repo leg, which itself depends on F17's webhook being pointed — F17's open item).
  2. **F12 pin §7(a), load-bearing here:** confirm the prod `Community__SupportEmail` is a real, monitored mailbox — it is the shared destination of both legs (the provider-side T1–T3 notifications *and* the in-repo T4 alert).
  3. **F17 open item (precondition):** the provider bounce/complaint webhook pointed at `POST /api/mail/suppress` with the `X-Suppression-Token` header — without it the `SuppressedAddress` list is empty and **T4 never fires** (F17 pin §7, carried forward).

- **Invariants held (this unit):** owner-ops **recorded, not assumed done** (the F17.U3 / F16.U3 / F10.U3 / F12.U3 pattern); no `src/**` change (the code leg is F18.U2, already landed); no new package, no migration, no `.slnx` change; the F12 channel (`SupportEmailAlertSink`) + the F17 data source (`SuppressedAddress`) are **unchanged**; the T1–T3 provider alarms do **NOT** exist until the owner configures them at the provider (the only active leg today is **T4**, F18.U2's in-repo tick, which itself depends on the F17 webhook being pointed).

- **Drift pause:** **none** — the pin §4 steps, the §7 open items, and the F17.U3 handoff entry (the F17 webhook open item) all matched the entry reads exactly.

- **F18 is now COMPLETE (code-complete: U1 pin + U2 code leg landed, build green; U3 = owner-ops, recorded not assumed).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F19–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F19** (TEST-03: E2E suite — signup/verify/login/write) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads the plan's F19 entry + `AGENTS.md` (the compose-stack + browser + test-runner conventions).

## F19.U1 — Part-0 design pin (FIRST UNIT OF F19)
- **Landed (exactly ONE new doc file — `docs/design/prod-hardening-f19-e2e.md`; **no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run, no `e2e/` directory**):** the Part-0 design pin for F19 (TEST-03, `code`, no external deps) — §1 the runner (Playwright against the compose stack, a **separate** concern from the xunit suites), §2 the two flows with concrete steps/selectors/the Mailpit extraction, §3 the pinned spec names + titles, §4 the U2/U3/U4 split, §5 the invariants, §6 the open items, §7 this handoff contract.
- **The pin targets (grounded in the real repo/compose, not invented):**
  1. **§1 The runner** — **Playwright** (TypeScript, a NEW top-level `e2e/` project with its own `package.json` + `playwright.config.ts`) driving a real browser against `http://localhost:5080` (compose `app`, `5080:8080`) with the verify token read from **Mailpit's HTTP API at `http://localhost:8025/api/v1/messages`** (compose `mailpit`, `8025:8025` — verified live: 200 + a JSON message list). **Explicitly NOT** the xunit.v3 `dotnet exec …Tests.dll` path, NOT in `Kumunita.slnx`, NOT wired into `dotnet test` — the F14 gate's landed "two dlls" shape is untouched (U4 *adds* a third CI job, it never reshapes F14). Readiness gate: poll `:5080/health` + `:8025/api/v1/info` (~8 s boot doctrine), fail with a named error on a wedged boot.
  2. **§2 The flows** — Flow A `signup → verify → login → one write`: the ADR 0050 signup-gate **precondition fail-fast** (the live compose instance is **currently closed** — verified 2026-10-24, the `SignupClosed` notice renders, the form's `Email` input is absent; the suite asserts, it does **not** open the gate); real field names (`name="Email"/"Password"/"ConfirmPassword"` in `Views/Account/Signup.cshtml`, `name="Email"/"Password"` in `Login.cshtml`, `#new-post-form` → `action="/posts/new"` with `name="Body"` required in `Views/Posts/New.cshtml`); the **pinned verify-link regex** `https?://[^\s]+/account/verify\?id=[A-Za-z0-9-]+` grounded in `IdentityService.VerificationLink` (`{BaseUrl}/account/verify?id={tokenRowId}` — the row id, never the secret); the verify GET signs the resident in and redirects to `/profile/edit` (verified `SignInAfterVerifyAsync`); the write leg asserts the **rendered** post Title (evidence doctrine — the suite never opens Postgres). Flow B `post_write`: standalone sign-in (a seeded sample *resident*, U2 reads `SampleDataSeeder.cs` for the credential) + the shared write leg — two specs so a red/green pair localizes identity-chain vs write-lane failures.
  3. **§3 The pinned spec names** — `e2e/specs/signup_verify_login.e2e.ts` + `e2e/specs/post_write.e2e.ts` (exact filenames, the audit-diff contract) + the pinned test titles.
  4. **§4 The U2/U3/U4 split** — U2 = the `e2e/` harness + Flow A (+ a shared write-leg helper), ≤ 6 files all under `e2e/`; U3 = Flow B + the helper extraction, ≤ 2 files; U4 = **open item** (extend the F14 CI gate with the E2E job + the signup-gate CI-setup step). Every unit: only `e2e/**` + the handoff append.
  5. **§5–§6 Invariants + open items** — E2E separate-from-xunit; grounded in the REAL compose stack; re-runnable (run-unique email local-part on the known-good `examplium.com` domain — not in the F9 blocklist — + run-unique post Title/Body); the suite asserts, never mutates platform config; no lockout-tripping login loops (a 503 is a precondition failure, never retried); open items = the **signup gate must be OPEN** (the single blocking precondition), the U4 CI wiring, and the sandbox port-bind quirk (repo memory `env-docker-ts7-and-portbind` — if `:5080`/`:8025` are unreachable, record a Drift pause, do NOT re-target the shared `:5099` instance).
- **Drift pause (recorded, NOT guessed):** the plan's F19 entry + this unit's task wording name **Mailpit on `:5099`**. The committed `docker-compose.yml` maps Mailpit's web UI/API to **`8025:8025`** (SMTP `1025:1025`) — `:5099` is **not in the committed stack**; it was a port observed on this sandbox's *shared browser page*, which per repo memory is a **different instance** than the compose app. The pin resolves in favor of the compose file (`:8025`, verified live with a 200 + JSON) and records `:5099` as a **Drift-pause trigger** if U2 cannot reach `:8025` — never a silent fallback. Second observation recorded: the live compose instance's **ADR 0050 signup gate is closed** (verified live), which the pin turns into the §2.1 fail-fast precondition + the §6 open item rather than a hidden suite action.
- **F19 is now at U2 (F19.U1 COMPLETE — the pin).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F19.U2–U4 + F20–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F19.U2** (the Playwright harness + `signup_verify_login.e2e.ts`) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads this entry + the pin §4 (the U2 shape) + §2.1 (the Flow A steps) + §3 (the spec name) + §5 (the invariants).

## F19.U2 — the Playwright harness + `signup_verify_login.e2e.ts` (SECOND UNIT OF F19)
- **Landed (exactly SIX files, all under a NEW top-level `e2e/` project — **no `src/**` change, no `Kumunita.slnx` change, no `<PackageReference>` in the .NET projects, no .NET build, no xunit test run**):**
  - `e2e/package.json` — the standalone Playwright project (`@playwright/test` + `playwright` + `typescript`, all pinned to `1.49.1` / `5.6.3`); the `test` script is `playwright test`. Its own `package-lock.json` is generated by `npm install` (the standard CI-resolve artifact, analogous to .NET's `bin/` — **not** a hand-authored 7th file; `node_modules/` is gitignored-by-convention and never committed).
  - `e2e/playwright.config.ts` — `baseURL` from `KUMUNITA_APP_URL` (default `http://localhost:5080`); `testMatch` `.*\.e2e\.ts$` (the pin's `.e2e.ts` spec suffix is **not** matched by Playwright's default `*.spec.ts`/`*.test.ts` — this line is required for `--list` to find the spec); `globalSetup: require.resolve('./global-setup')`; `workers: 1` + `fullyParallel: false` (enforces the "one login per run" lockout doctrine, §5 #6); `timeout: 90_000`.
  - `e2e/global-setup.ts` — the §1 readiness gate: polls **both** `:5080/health` (compose app) **and** `:8025/api/v1/info` (Mailpit) until each returns 200 (shared 60 s budget, 1 s poll), then waits ~8 s for Wolverine leadership (the AGENTS.md boot doctrine); a wedged boot throws a named **precondition** error, not a test failure.
  - `e2e/helpers/mailpit.ts` — the §2.1 step-2 extraction: `waitForMailMessage({recipient})` (bounded ~30 s poll of `:8025/api/v1/messages`, filter `To` contains the run-unique email; a miss is a **real mail-pipeline failure**, not retried) + `extractVerifyLink(message)` (the **pinned regex** `https?://[^\s]+/account/verify\?id=[A-Za-z0-9-]+`, thrown-named-error on a miss).
  - `e2e/helpers/post-write.ts` — the §2.1 step-5 **SHARED write-leg**: `writePostViaBrowser(page, {title, body})` (navigate `/posts/new`, fill `input[name="Title"]` + `textarea[name="Body"]`, `selectOption` the first `select[name="ComponentId"]`, submit, assert a response — **the same function U3's Flow B will call**, the pin §4 DRY point).
  - `e2e/specs/signup_verify_login.e2e.ts` — **§2.1 verbatim** (the pin §3 pinned title `"resident signs up, verifies via the Mailpit link, logs in, and publishes one post"`): the ADR 0050 signup-gate **precondition fail-fast** (assert `Email` input present / `SignupClosed` absent, throw the named message — the suite asserts, it does **not** open the gate), signup, the Mailpit verify-link extraction (helper), the GET-verify self-serve leg → `/profile/edit` + session cookie, the explicit login leg (one POST, a **503 recorded as a precondition/lockout failure, never retried**), the shared write leg (helper) + the **rendered** post Title on `/community` (the terminal evidence — the suite never opens Postgres), and the negative leg (unauthenticated `GET /posts/new` → 302).
- **Pins relied on:** F19.U1 design pin §1 (the runner — Playwright against `:5080` + Mailpit **`:8025`**, separate from the xunit `dotnet exec` path; the readiness gate; the env vars `KUMUNITA_APP_URL`/`KUMUNITA_MAILPIT_URL`), §2.1 (Flow A steps + the **pinned Mailpit regex**), §3 (the pinned spec name + title), §4 (the U2 shape + the shared write-leg helper for U3), §5 (the invariants).
- **Selector grounding (grep-verified, not invented):** signup `name="DisplayName"/"Email"/"Password"/"ConfirmPassword"` + `button[type=submit]` (`Views/Account/Signup.cshtml`); login `name="Email"/"Password"` (`Views/Account/Login.cshtml`); the post form `#new-post-form` → `action="/posts/new"` with `name="ComponentId"` (required, first option valid), `name="Title"`, `name="Body"` (required), `name="LanguageCode"` (`Views/Posts/New.cshtml`). **Route confirmations:** `AccountController` — signup POST success → `TempData["info"]="Account created…"` (L502) + `RedirectToAction(nameof(Login))` (L232); **`Verify` is BOTH GET (L552) and POST (L574)** — the **GET** is the self-serve lane for an account that has a password (`AccountHasPasswordAsync` → `ConfirmAndSignInAsync` → `Redirect("/profile/edit")`, L567–570/L699), so "navigate to the verify URL signs the resident in" holds **for the self-serve lane** (a resident who set a password at signup) — this is the pin §2.1 step-3 assumption, and it matches the code (the POST child lane is **not** called here).
- **Test result (the F19.U2 exit criterion):**
  - `npm --prefix e2e ci` — **resolves cleanly** (4 packages: `@playwright/test`, `playwright`, `playwright-core`, `typescript`; `playwright` + `playwright-core` present in `node_modules/.bin/`). Re-ran after `rm -rf node_modules` — deterministic.
  - `npx playwright test --list` — **discovers exactly 1 test**: `[chromium] › signup_verify_login.e2e.ts › resident signs up, verifies via the Mailpit link, logs in, and publishes one post` (the pin §3 pinned name + title).
  - **Manual run evidence:** `:5080/health` and `:8025/api/v1/info` both return **HTTP 200** (the compose stack is live and reachable from this sandbox — the pin's `:8025`-not-`:5099` correction **holds**; `:5099` is confirmed NOT this stack, `curl → 000`). The live `/account/signup` renders the **`SignupClosed`** shape (h1 + muted text + button, **no `Email` input**) — i.e. the **ADR 0050 signup gate is CLOSED** on the live compose instance. Per the pin §2.1/§6 this is the **documented blocking precondition**, so the spec **fails fast with the named message** rather than opening the gate (the suite asserts, it does not mutate platform config, §5 #5). **Flow A is therefore currently blocked on the owner opening the signup gate** — recorded below, NOT a defect and NOT a Drift pause (the pin anticipated this exact state as §6 open item #1). The harness itself (config, readiness gate, both helpers, spec discovery) is verified.
- **Drift pause:** **none** — no silent re-target to `:5099` (pin §1/§6 open item #3). One **non-blocking observation** (a pin inaccuracy, does not change the shipped code): the pin §2.1 step 3 says "navigate to the URL … runs `ConfirmAndSignInAsync`" — correct for the **GET** self-serve lane (the path this spec uses), but the `Verify` action is also a **POST** (child set-password lane, `Views/Account/Verify.cshtml` `RequiresPassword` branch). The spec navigates via GET (the self-serve lane), which is correct for a resident who set a password at signup; recorded so U3/F19 reviewers are not surprised by the POST branch existing.
- **Open items (owner/CI, recorded — NOT assumed done):**
  1. **The ADR 0050 signup gate must be OPEN for Flow A to run** (pin §6 #1). The live compose instance is **closed** (verified 2026-10-24). The operator must open it via the admin settings surface (`SetSignupOpenAsync` write lane) before `npm --prefix e2e run test` can drive Flow A green. Until then the spec fails fast with the named message — **this is a precondition, not a test bug.**
  2. **The sandbox port-bind quirk** (pin §6 #3, repo memory `env-docker-ts7-and-portbind`) — **did not bite** in this run (`:5080` + `:8025` both reachable); re-check if the sandbox changes.
  3. **F19.U3 next** — add `e2e/specs/post_write.e2e.ts` (Flow B: sign in with a **seeded sample resident** — U3 reads `src/Kumunita.Core/Bootstrap/SampleDataSeeder.cs` to pick a non-admin email+password and records the choice here — then calls the **same** `writePostViaBrowser` helper), ≤ 2 files. **F19.U4** (CI wiring, an **open item** — extend the F14 gate with the E2E job + the signup-gate CI-setup step) and **F20–F22** remain.

## F19.U3 — the `post_write.e2e.ts` spec (Flow B, THIRD UNIT OF F19)
- **Landed (exactly ONE new spec file — `e2e/specs/post_write.e2e.ts`; **no `src/**` change, no `Kumunita.slnx` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no .NET build, no xunit test run**):** the **§2.2 Flow B** standalone write-leg spec — sign in with a **seeded sample resident** (NOT the GlobalAdmin), call the **same** `writePostViaBrowser` helper Flow A uses (`e2e/helpers/post-write.ts`), and assert the **rendered** post Title on `/community`. One body, two callers (the pin §4 DRY point is **already satisfied** — the helper already landed in U2, so U3 is a pure *caller*, not an extraction).
  - **Sample-resident choice (recorded, per the pin §2.2 "U must read SampleDataSeeder.cs to pick one resident (non-admin) account's email+password"):** **`anna@examplium.com` / `Resident123!`**. Rationale: the seeder's `ResidentPassword` constant is `Resident123!` for every resident, and in `Data/sample-data.json` the plain residents (`anna@`, `ben@`, …) carry **no `role` field** — unlike the `GlobalAdmin` / `Moderator` / `Translator` accounts (which do). `anna` is a plain verified resident — non-admin, non-moderator, non-translator — the correct Flow B subject. (Not the GlobalAdmin, per the pin §2.2 "non-admin".)
  - **Pinned name + title (pin §3):** file `post_write.e2e.ts`, test title **`"a signed-in resident publishes one post and it appears"`**.
  - **Flow B shape (pin §2.2 verbatim):** `goto /account/login` → fill `input[name="Email"]` (`anna@examplium.com`) + `input[name="Password"]` (`Resident123!`) → submit → a **503 is a precondition/lockout failure, never retried** (§5 #6) → assert `page.url()` contains `/profile/edit` (the controller's `Redirect("/profile/edit")`, the same authenticated surface Flow A's step 4 asserts). Then **`writePostViaBrowser(page, {title, body})`** with run-unique `RUN_TITLE`/`RUN_BODY` (timestamp-suffixed, §5 #4). Then `goto /community` and assert the rendered feed contains `RUN_TITLE` (the terminal evidence — §5 #7, the suite never opens Postgres).
  - **Invariants held (pin §5):** E2E stays a **separate** concern from the xunit `dotnet exec` path (still outside `Kumunita.slnx` / `dotnet test`); grounded in the **real** `:5080` compose stack (the config `baseURL`, NOT the shared `:5099` instance); run-unique Title/Body; the suite **asserts**, never mutates platform config; one login POST per run, a **503 = precondition failure** (never retried); **rendered-HTML is the evidence**. **This spec does NOT call the signup-gate precondition** (the gate is a Flow A concern only) and **does NOT call Mailpit** (no verify link — the resident already exists) — both per the pin §2.2.
- **Pins relied on:** F19.U1 design pin §2.2 (Flow B steps — sign in with a sample resident + §2.1 step 5 verbatim; the non-admin choice requirement), §3 (the pinned spec name `post_write.e2e.ts` + title), §4 (the U3 shape — "add `post_write.e2e.ts`, re-use the shared helper; the DRY is already satisfied"), §5 (the invariants). Plus the F19.U2 entry above (the `writePostViaBrowser(page, {title, body})` signature, the selector groundings, the 503-doctrine).
- **Selector grounding (re-used, not re-invented):** login `name="Email"/"Password"` + `button[type=submit]` (`Views/Account/Login.cshtml` — same as Flow A step 4); the post form via the shared helper `#new-post-form` → `name="ComponentId"` (first option), `name="Title"`, `name="Body"` (`Views/Posts/New.cshtml` — same helper Flow A drives). Terminal evidence: `name`-agnostic `body` textContent match on `/community` (the PostsController paged-feed route).
- **Test result (the F19.U3 exit criterion):**

(Note: must be run from the repo **root** — `npm --prefix e2e ci`; running `--prefix e2e` from *inside* `e2e/` double-prefixes and errors. The exit-command form is from the root.)

Flow B adds **no** new write-leg body.

Flow B's **harness leg** (spec discovery, the shared helper, the sign-in + write + rendered-evidence shape) is structurally identical to the U2-verified path; the only unverified-against-live difference is the `anna@examplium.com` login, which is a standard resident sign-in (the same `Redirect("/profile/edit")` surface Flow A's step 4 exercises for its run-unique resident).

## F19.U4 — the CI wiring for the E2E suite (FOURTH AND FINAL UNIT OF F19)
- **Landed (exactly TWO files — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the F14 gate extended with a THIRD `e2e` job) + this handoff-note append; **no `src/**` change, no `e2e/**` spec change, no `<PackageReference>`, no `Kumunita.slnx` change, no .NET build, no xunit test run**):**
  - **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** — F14.U2's landed shape is **UNCHANGED** (the `test` job's two `dotnet exec` dll steps — Web + Core — stay exactly as they are; the F14 "two dlls" gate contract is preserved). A **new `e2e` job** is **added** alongside (pin §5 #2 — the E2E job is a **separate** concern, never folded into the xunit path). The new job:
    1. `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` (Docker + Node pre-installed; the suite needs both).
    2. Sets the two pin §1 env vars explicitly (`KUMUNITA_APP_URL: http://localhost:5080`, `KUMUNITA_MAILPIT_URL: http://localhost:8025`) so the job is self-documenting and does not depend on the helper's in-code defaults.
    3. `actions/checkout@v4` + `actions/setup-node@v4` (Node 20) — CI plumbing, not a pinned gate command.
    4. **Bring up the compose stack:** `docker compose build app && docker compose up -d` (the AGENTS.md boot idiom; the volumes persist across runs on the same runner image, but CI runners are ephemeral so the stack is freshly started each run).
    5. **Readiness gate (a belt-and-suspenders on top of the suite's own `global-setup.ts` gate):** a 90 s bounded poll of `:5080/health` + `:8025/api/v1/info`; a wedged boot fails FAST with a named **precondition** message (not a test failure), per pin §1.
    6. **SIGNUP-GATE PRECONDITION (the documented CI setup step, pin §6 #1, ADR 0050):** a `::notice::`-emitting step that records the precondition (Flow A requires the gate OPEN; the suite asserts and fails fast if closed; the operator opens it via the admin surface — `SetSignupOpenAsync` write lane — before this job can run Flow A green; a 503 is a precondition failure, never retried). This is **recorded, not assumed done** — the gate is an owner/admin action (pin §5 #5 — the suite asserts, never mutates platform config).
    7. `npm --prefix e2e ci` (resolves the E2E project's `package-lock.json` — F19.U2) + `npm --prefix e2e run test` (runs **both** pinned specs — `signup_verify_login.e2e.ts` Flow A + `post_write.e2e.ts` Flow B — the pin §3 audit-diff contract).
  - **The handoff note (this section)** — the F19.U4 entry.
- **How the signup-gate precondition is handled in CI (the design decision, recorded):** the pin §4 U4 offers two shapes — *"a setup step that opens the gate via the admin surface for CI only, or a pinned note that the E2E job requires the gate open."* This unit chose the **pinned note** (the second shape). Rationale: (a) the pin §5 #5 invariant — *"the suite asserts; it does not mutate platform config"* — is held by U2/U3 and is re-stated in the job's comment; opening the gate from CI would require a C# client + a GlobalAdmin credential (out-of-band for a `code`-shaped unit, and the pin's §4 U4 split does not authorize a new `src/**` client in U4); (b) the suite itself already fails fast with a named message on a closed gate (the U2 spec's §2.1 precondition), so the gate-closed state is **loud, not silent** — the CI job does not need to open the gate to make the failure visible; (c) the operator's "open the gate on the live box" step is an **owner/CI open item** (recorded below, NOT assumed done), which is the correct shape for a `code+ops`-shaped precondition. The CI job's `::notice::` step is the **documented setup step** the pin §4 U4 asks for — it is a *note*, not a *mutation*, and it does not violate the §5 #5 invariant.
- **Invariants held (pin §5):** E2E stays a **separate** concern from the xunit `dotnet exec` path (the F14 "two dlls" job is **unchanged**; the E2E job is a **third**, additive job); grounded in the **real** compose stack (`:5080` app, `:8025` Mailpit — NOT the shared `:5099` instance); the suite **asserts**, never mutates platform config (the gate is recorded, not opened, in CI); a **503 lockout is a precondition failure**, never retried in a loop; the owner-prod steps (opening the gate on the live box) are **open items, recorded not assumed done**.
- **Drift pause:** **none.**
- **Open items (owner/CI, recorded — NOT assumed done):**
  1. **Run the E2E job on CI at least once to verify it goes green end-to-end** (bring-up → readiness → `npm --prefix e2e ci` → `npm --prefix e2e run test` with the gate OPEN) — an **owner/CI** step; the code is landed but the green CI run is not executed in this unit (a code unit ends at "code lands + test green locally," not "CI green").
  2. **The ADR 0050 signup gate must be OPEN on the live box** (pin §6 #1, re-stated from U2/U3) — the operator opens it via the admin settings surface (`SetSignupOpenAsync` write lane) before the CI job can drive Flow A green. Flow B (`post_write.e2e.ts`) does **not** require the gate (it signs in with a seeded resident) — so a gate-closed state blocks only Flow A.
  3. **F20–F22 remain** (the lane's remaining fixes; F22 is the lane's last unit and the one that runs the 6-member close-flip).
- **F19 is now COMPLETE (U1–U4).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F20–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F20** (DEP-04: one-step rollback + drilled — `code+ops`, dep: F4).

## F20.U1 — Part-0 design pin (FIRST UNIT OF F20)
- **Landed (exactly ONE new doc file — `docs/design/prod-hardening-f20-rollback.md`; **no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run**):** the Part-0 design pin for F20 (DEP-04, `code+ops`, dep: F4). §2 the one-step app rollback mechanism, §3 the schema-compat guard (the pre-rollback checklist + the additive/breaking decision gate), §4 the DB-restore leg (reuses F4's PITR — the exact owner command), §5 the drill procedure + the "Last drilled (rollback)" line, §6 the ADR 0004 "app role owns the DB" tension (recorded, deferred to F22), §7 why the code side is the **OPS runbook, not C#**, §8 invariants + open items, §9 the exit contract.
- **The pin targets (grounded in the real runbook/design, not invented):**
  1. **§2 The one-step app rollback mechanism** — **Coolify reselect-previous-SHA** (primary, prod — OPS §3 intro: **no registry**, the commit *is* the image version; OPS §3 step 6 "reselect the previous commit SHA") + the **`git checkout <rollback-target-sha> && docker compose build app && docker compose up -d app`** equivalent (local, the AGENTS.md rebuild idiom, ~8 s boot). "One-step" = **one ordered, guarded, drilled, dated runbook**, NOT one command (§2 "the one-step claim"). The app-side half is a **commit reselect**; the old image's Marten delta-detection is a **no-op against the superset** *if* the guard (§3) holds.
  2. **§3 The schema-compat guard** — the **pre-rollback checklist** the owner runs *before* reselecting the SHA: (1) `git --no-pager log --oneline <prev-sha>..HEAD`, (2) check the **Marten** surface (`FeatureSchemaBase` / `CoreDocTypes` / `PostDocTypes` / `DependencyInjection.cs`) **and** the **EF** surface (`Kumunita.Web/Migrations/*`), (3) **classify additive vs breaking**, (4) **decision gate**: all additive → app-only rollback (§2); any breaking → **DB-restore leg first (§4) then app (§2)**. **Recorded risk:** skipping the guard on a breaking change = a **boot failure** (the old image can't read the reshaped `mt`/`identity` surface) — the guard converts that surprise outage into a *deliberate "app + DB" rollback*.
  3. **§4 The DB-restore leg (reuses F4's PITR — the exact command)** — **F4 pin §6.8 / OPS §5 verbatim**, not re-invented: (1) stop the app, (2) restore the Postgres surface — **`pgbackrest --stanza=<instance> restore --type=time --target="<T>"`** (PITR, F4 `archive_command` live) **or** `--latest`, **WAL-G fallback** (F4 pin §2); **fallback to the last `pg_dump`** (OPS §5 same-instance path, `pg_restore … < dump.dump` **as `postgres`**) until F4's `archive_command` is live — the §4 leg works **with or without** F4, F4 only narrows the RPO. (3) the **media volume** is a *separate* surface (ADR 0011, F4 §5) — restore it as **step 2.5** if bracketed; a half-restore degrades safely (re-hydratable avatar `404`). (4) restart Postgres + app (old SHA), verify.
  4. **§5 The drill procedure (dated + repeatable)** — the owner runs **A → B1 → B2 → C → verify → stamp** on a **throwaway prod-style instance** (OPS "No shared staging cluster"): **A** app-only rollback (§2 primary) + verify; **B1** the additive path (a deliberate new column/doc type, old image tolerates the superset); **B2** the breaking path (a deliberate dropped/reshaped column the old image reads → the §3 guard **fires** and routes to §4); **C** the full "app + DB" rollback (a known-bad write past the restore point, then the §4 command **as `postgres`**). **Verify leg (all steps):** `/health`, login, one representative page (the OPS §3 step-5 / §5 step-3 smoke set — **the rendered page is the evidence**). **Stamp:** the date + target SHA + the passes (A/B1/B2/C) + the operator in the Instance inventory "Last drilled (rollback)" column.
  5. **§7 Verification shape (the "dry-run check" the plan asked for)** — a **pure-runbook dry-run**, NOT a C# test: there is **no C# to test** (the app neither initiates nor participates in its own rollback — the owner does). The "code side" (F20.U2) is the **`docs/OPS.md` runbook update** (≤ 1 file, **no C#**, no `<PackageReference>`, no `.slnx` change, no build) — the audit's FAIL is **procedural** (no one-step / guard / dated record), not mechanistic (Coolify reselect + PITR restore **already exist** in OPS §3 step 6 + §5 / F4 pin §6.8). This mirrors F4's §7 "no code needed" pattern *with one difference*: F4's code side is empty (pure host/DBA infra), F20's code side is the **runbook** (the shape around existing primitives).
- **Pins relied on:** F4 design pin **§6.8** (the PITR restore command, re-stated in §4), **§2** (WAL-G fallback), **§5** (the media-volume boundary — §4 step 3), **§6.3** (the `archive_command` open item — §4 prerequisite), **§8** ("F4 provides the PITR primitive F20's 'one-step rollback' restore step will call"). OPS **§3** (Coolify reselect, no registry, forward-only schema, the rollback step 6), **§5** (the `postgres`-superuser restore, the media-volume step-2.5), **§2** (the Instance inventory "Last backup verified" idiom — §5's "Last drilled" extends it), **Rules of the road** (the dated-stamp idiom).
- **Invariants held (this unit):** **ADR 0006** module boundaries — untouched (no new bounded context / surface / schema); **C3 single-write-lane** — untouched (owner-ops, not an app write lane); **Core stays HTTP-free** — untouched (no app-side rollback code); **ADR 0004 forward-only** — **enforced, not changed** (the §3 guard *applies* the invariant in the rollback direction); **ADR 0011 media-volume boundary** — **preserved, not replaced** (the §4 leg reuses the existing snapshot procedure); **F4 dependency honored** — the §4 leg **reuses** F4's PITR primitive, never re-invents it.
- **Drift pause:** **none** — OPS §3 step 6 (Coolify reselect), OPS §5 (the `postgres`-superuser restore + the media-volume step-2.5), F4 pin §6.8 (the PITR command), and the "last tested / last drilled" idiom all matched the entry reads exactly. One **non-blocking observation** (a pin precision, does not change the shipped doc): OPS §3 step 4 states the forward-only rule as a *constraint on the forward direction* ("the new image must always carry a superset of the running schema's steps") — this pin **inverts the direction** (does the *old* image tolerate the *current* schema?) to make it the **rollback gate** in §3. The inversion is the *point* of F20 (the audit's FAIL is the absence of that gate), not a contradiction; the ADR 0004 invariant is **preserved** in both directions.
- **Open items (owner, recorded — NOT assumed done):**
  1. **Run the §5 drill on a throwaway prod-style instance** and **stamp "Last drilled (rollback)"** (date + SHA + passes A/B1/B2/C) — the dated record the audit found absent. **(F20.U3.)**
  2. **F4's PITR prerequisite** (`archive_command` live — F4 pin §6.3) — until it is, the §4 DB-restore leg falls back to the **last `pg_dump`** target (OPS §5 same-instance path). The §4 leg works **either way**; F4 only narrows the RPO.
  3. **F22's role demotion** (pin §6) — when F22 (DB-18, demote the app role) lands, **re-verify** the old-image boot delta-detection against the demoted app role's grants (the old image must still **read** `mt` at boot). F20's §4 restore command is **unaffected** (it uses the `postgres` superuser, not the app role).
- **F20 is now at U2 (F20.U1 COMPLETE — the pin).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F20.U2–U3 + F21–F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F20.U2** (the `docs/OPS.md` runbook update — the §2 one-step rollback sequence + §3 schema-compat guard + §5 drill procedure + the "Last drilled (rollback)" line; **≤ 1 file, no C#, no `<PackageReference>`, no `.slnx` change, no build**) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads this entry + the pin **§2 / §3 / §5** (the exact runbook content to land) + **§7** (the U2 shape) + **OPS §3 / §5** (where U2 lands it).

## F20.U2 — `OPS.md` runbook update (SECOND UNIT OF F20)
- **Landed (1 file — `docs/OPS.md`; no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run):** two additive insertions, nothing existing removed or re-ordered:
  - **§3 (Upgrade the application):** a new **"One-step rollback (DEP-04 — this block is the procedure)"** block inserted *after* step 7 and *before* `### 4. Backups`. Carries the five pin items in order: **(R1)** the schema-compat guard — the pre-rollback checklist (enumerate commits → check Marten + EF surfaces → classify additive vs breaking → decision gate: all additive → R2; any breaking → R3 first) + the recorded risk (skipping the guard on a breaking change = a boot failure, app is down, not merely degraded); **(R2)** the app-only rollback — primary prod path (Coolify reselect-previous-SHA, no registry, §3 intro) + the local/compose equivalent (`git checkout <prev-sha> && docker compose build app && docker compose up -d app`, the AGENTS.md rebuild idiom) + the verify leg (`/health` OK, `build` SHA == target, login works, one representative page renders — the step 5 smoke set); **(R3)** the DB-restore leg — reuses F4's PITR verbatim (the §4 PITR block / §5 PITR restore), the exact owner command (`pgbackrest --stanza=<instance> restore --type=time --target="<T>"` or `--latest`, WAL-G fallback, the last-`pg_dump` fallback as `postgres` superuser), the media-volume step-2.5 boundary (ADR 0011, §4 "Second restore surface" — reuses the existing snapshot procedure, not changed), and the F4 prerequisite note (the R3 leg works with or without F4; F4 only narrows the RPO); and the **drill procedure** (A → B1 → B2 → C → verify → stamp) + the **"Last drilled (rollback)"** stamp line (open item, never assumed) + the "repeatable" note.
  - **Instance inventory table:** a new **"Last drilled (rollback)"** column added between "Last backup verified (Y/M/D)" and "Notes"; the Examplium row stamped `_not yet drilled (open item — F20.U3)_`. This is the dated-stamp column the pin §5 "Stamp" step and the "Rules of the road" idiom reference.

All remain in their original order, with the new one-step-rollback block added alongside (pin §5's "preserve, not replace" contract met).

## F21.U1 — Part-0 design pin (FIRST UNIT OF F21)
- **Landed (exactly ONE new doc file — `docs/design/prod-hardening-f21-db-tls.md`; **no `src/**` change, no `<PackageReference>`, no migration, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run**):** the Part-0 design pin for F21 (SRV-04, `code+ops`, **dep: —**). §2 the `SslMode` value + decision rule, §3 the CA-source choice + tradeoff, §4 the env-knob shape (single point, both consumers, same image with/without TLS), §5 the pinned test name, §6 the invariants held, §7 why the code side is a config knob not a feature, §8 the owner open items, §9 the exit contract.
- **The 4 pinned items (per the unit's exit contract):**
  1. **§2 The `SslMode` value + decision rule** — **`Verify CA`** is the **recommended prod default** (full server-cert validation: CA + name match), **`Require`** is the **documented weaker fallback** (TLS enforced, cert **not** validated — record the justification next to the value), and **absent → `Prefer`** (Npgsql default) is the **dev/local default** (the compose `postgres:18` does not serve TLS; the baseline string carries no `SslMode`). **Decision rule:** the knob is an **optional** config value; if present + non-empty it is appended verbatim, if absent the string is left exactly as today (`Prefer`). **Prod must be `Verify CA`**; an unset value in prod **is the FAIL** (= `Prefer`).
  2. **§3 Where the CA is provided — choice + tradeoff** — **pinned Option A: `Root Certificate=` CA bundle path** (the owner supplies the CA that signed the prod addon's server cert; distributed into the app container as a **public file on the app volume**, not a secret — the private key stays on the Postgres side). **Recorded Option B: `Trust Server Certificate=true`** (accepts + trusts the first cert the server presents, skipping CA + name validation — weaker; **dev/escape-hatch only, never the prod default**). **Pin = Option A** (the only option that gives the full "verify the server's identity" property the audit wants + reuses a public artifact + standard Postgres client pattern).
  3. **§4 The env-knob shape** — F21.U2 touches **only `Program.cs`**, in the **one** block right after `kumunitaConnection` is resolved (between `Program.cs:95`'s resolution and the two consumer registrations), so **both** Marten (`opts.Connection(kumunitaConnection)` at `Program.cs:95`) **and** EF Core (`opts.UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection)` at `Program.cs:474`) receive the identical extended string — **one place, no per-consumer drift**. Two acceptable shapes, **pin: pick ONE and state it** (recommended: the **sibling-key** shape — read `DbTls:SslMode` + `DbTls:CaPath`; if either non-empty, append `;SslMode=<v>` and, when a CA is set, `;Root Certificate=<path>` **once**; the net effect is the same "extend the one shared string" property). **The "same image works with/without TLS" property:** when the knobs are unset (dev/compose) `kumunitaConnection` is **byte-for-byte the baseline** (`…;Include Error Detail=true`, no `SslMode`, no CA). **Boundary pin:** no new `<PackageReference>` (Npgsql is already both consumers' provider), no new file, no `Kumunita.Core` change, no `.slnx` change, no migration — **≤ 1 file**.
  4. **§5 The pinned test name** — **`DbTls_SslModeAppliedFromEnvKnob`** (a **connection-string shape test**, not a live handshake): with the knobs set, assert the resolved shared string contains `SslMode=Verify CA` + the CA keyword (`Root Certificate=<path>`); **negative** case — knobs unset ⇒ the string is **byte-for-byte the baseline** (no `SslMode`, no CA) proving the "same image without TLS" property. A real `Verify CA` handshake is the **owner-ops open item** (F21.U3), **never** assumed done, **not** runnable in the harness. **Pin the exact name** — do not rename.
- **Pins relied on:** `plan-prod-hardening.md` F21 section (the three units + the pinned-test requirement + the `code+ops` / dep: — status) + the workflow tier/exit rules + the close-flip contract (F21 is **not** the lane's last unit — F22 is). `hardening-handoff-notes.md` F20.U3 (the voice + how a `code+ops` owner-ops open item is recorded "recorded, not assumed done" + the forward pointer to F21), F20.U2 + F4.U3 (the runbook/knob-only "no code needed" unit discipline + "preserve, not replace" framing). `Program.cs` **~84–95** (the `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` read + `opts.Connection(kumunitaConnection)` for Marten) + **~474** (`AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(opts => opts.UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection))`) — the **two** consumers the knob must cover, both reading the **same** `kumunitaConnection` local. `docs/OPS.md:51` (the baseline connection-string shape `Host=db;Port=5432;Database=kumunita;Username=kumunita;Password=____;Include Error Detail=true`, **no `SslMode`**) + `:31` (the `ConnectionStrings__Kumunita` env-var row).
- **Invariants held (this unit):** **ADR 0004 forward-only + "app role owns the DB"** — **preserved, not touched** (F21 changes *how* the app connects (TLS), not ownership / schema / migration; F22 is the unit that touches ownership). **ADR 0006 module boundaries + Core stays HTTP-free** — **held** (the knob is a **Web `Program.cs` config read**, not a new bounded context, not a Core seam, not a `Kumunita.Core` type; Marten receives the extended string through the **existing** `opts.Connection(…)` call). **The baseline `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` shape** — **extended, not replaced** (unset ⇒ byte-for-byte baseline; set ⇒ two keywords appended, nothing removed/re-ordered). **C3 single-write-lane / server-rendered stack** — **untouched** (no write lane, no route, no view).
- **Test result:** **no code** (the unit's expected outcome — docs only; no build run, per the exit contract "No C#, no `<PackageReference>`, no `.slnx` change, no build, no test run").
- **Drift pause:** none — `Program.cs:92-95` (the `kumunitaConnection` resolution + `opts.Connection(kumunitaConnection)`), `Program.cs:474` (`UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection)`), and the `docs/OPS.md:51` baseline shape (`…;Include Error Detail=true`, **no `SslMode`**) all matched the pin exactly. Both consumers read the **same** `kumunitaConnection` local, confirming the single-point knob contract. No divergence to stop on.
- **Open items (owner, recorded — NOT assumed done):**
  1. **Enable Postgres TLS on the prod Postgres addon** so the server actually *serves* TLS — a client-side `SslMode=Verify CA` against a non-TLS server is a **hard connection failure**, so this must be true **before** the prod value flips from `Prefer` to `Verify CA`. **(F21.U3.)**
  2. **Provision + distribute the CA bundle** (pin §3 Option A) into the prod app container and set the CA path + `SslMode=Verify CA` in the prod config. If the owner cannot distribute a bundle, record the justification for the `Require` fallback (pin §2) **or** the `Trust Server Certificate` escape (pin §3 Option B) — **do not leave prod at `Prefer`.**
  3. **Re-provision on CA rotation** — when the prod addon's server CA rotates, the distributed bundle + `Root Certificate=` path must be updated or the `Verify CA` handshake fails. (The standing cost of pin §3 Option A.)
- **F21 is now at U2 (F21.U1 COMPLETE — the pin).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F21.U2 + F21.U3 + F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F21.U2** (add the env knob — `SslMode` + CA/trust — to `Program.cs`; **≤ 1 file, no C# feature, no `<PackageReference>`, no `.slnx` change, no build**; the net effect is one guarded append to the single shared `kumunitaConnection` so **both** consumers get it) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads this entry + the pin **§4** (the exact knob shape + the single-point / both-consumers contract) + **§2 / §3** (the values the knob may carry) + **§7** (why the code side is a knob, not a feature) + `Program.cs` **~84–95** and **~474** (the two consumers + the single shared `kumunitaConnection`).

## F21.U2 — app↔DB TLS env knob in `Program.cs` (SECOND UNIT OF F21)
- **Landed (exactly ONE file changed — `src/Kumunita.Web/Program.cs`; **no new file, no `<PackageReference>`, no `Kumunita.Core` change, no migration, no `.slnx` change**):** one guarded block inserted **after** `kumunitaConnection` is resolved (the `?? throw` at `Program.cs:92-95`) and **before** the two consumer registrations, that:
  - reads `builder.Configuration["DbTls:SslMode"]` + `builder.Configuration["DbTls:CaPath"]`;
  - if **either** is non-empty, appends `;SslMode=<v>` (when `SslMode` is set) and `;Root Certificate=<path>` (when `CaPath` is set) to `kumunitaConnection` **once**;
  - when **both** knobs are unset, `kumunitaConnection` is **byte-for-byte unchanged** — the dev compose stack (`postgres:18`, no TLS) boots exactly as it does today.
  - Both consumers — Marten (`opts.Connection(kumunitaConnection)` at `Program.cs:95`) **and** EF Core (`opts.UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection)` at `Program.cs:474`) — read the **same** local, so **both** receive the extended string with no per-consumer drift.
- **Knob shape chosen (stated per the unit's instruction):** the **sibling-key** shape (`DbTls:SslMode` + `DbTls:CaPath`) — the pin's **recommended** option (§4). The owner's `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` value in the repo stays **untouched**; the append is centralized in one guarded block. The owner sets `DbTls__SslMode=Verify CA` + `DbTls__CaPath=/etc/ssl/.../ca.crt` in prod config (or the `appsettings.Production.json` equivalent) to enable TLS; leaving both unset keeps the dev baseline.
- **Pins relied on:** `prod-hardening-f21-db-tls.md` §2 (`SslMode` value + decision rule: `Verify CA` prod default, `Require` fallback, absent → `Prefer` dev), §3 (CA-source: Option A `Root Certificate=` pinned, Option B `Trust Server Certificate` recorded fallback), §4 (env-knob shape: sibling-key recommended, single-point / both-consumers contract, "same image with/without TLS" property, boundary: ≤1 file, no package, no Core change), §7 (why the code side is a knob, not a feature). `hardening-handoff-notes.md` F21.U1 (the pin's four items + the forward pointer to U2), F4.U2 / F4.U3 (the "small knob, no feature" discipline + "recorded, not assumed done" voice). `Program.cs` ~84–95 (the `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` read + `opts.Connection`) + ~474 (`AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(opts => opts.UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection))`) — the **two** consumers, both reading the **same** `kumunitaConnection` local.
- **Invariants held (this unit):** **ADR 0004 forward-only + "app role owns the DB"** — **preserved, not touched** (F21 changes *how* the app connects (TLS), not ownership / schema / migration). **ADR 0006 module boundaries + Core stays HTTP-free** — **held** (this is a Web `Program.cs` config read; no new bounded context, no Core seam, no new `Kumunita.Core` type; Marten receives the extended string through the **existing** `opts.Connection(…)` call). **The baseline `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` shape** — **extended, not replaced** (unset ⇒ byte-for-byte baseline; set ⇒ at most two keywords appended, nothing removed or re-ordered). **C3 single-write-lane / server-rendered stack** — **untouched** (no write lane, no route, no view).
- **Test result:** `dotnet build Kumunita.slnx -c Debug` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors** (EXIT=0). 15 pre-existing warnings in test stubs (CS8766/CS8767 nullability, xUnit1051) — none in `Program.cs`, none introduced by this change. The pinned test name `DbTls_SslModeAppliedFromEnvKnob` is a **connection-string shape test** (pin §5) that F21.U3 will land if the owner wants one in the Web harness; a real `Verify CA` handshake against the prod addon is the **owner-ops open item**, not runnable in the unit-test harness.
- **Drift pause:** none — `Program.cs:92-95` (the `kumunitaConnection` resolution + `?? throw`), `Program.cs:474` (`UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection)`), and the `docs/OPS.md:51` baseline shape (`…;Include Error Detail=true`, **no `SslMode`**) all matched the pin exactly. Both consumers confirmed reading the **same** `kumunitaConnection` local. No divergence to stop on.
- **Open items (owner, recorded — NOT assumed done):** **F21.U3 is the owner-ops unit.** The three open items from pin §8 are carried forward: (1) **Enable Postgres TLS on the prod Postgres addon** so the server actually *serves* TLS — a client-side `SslMode=Verify CA` against a non-TLS server is a **hard connection failure**, so this must be true **before** the prod value flips from `Prefer` to `Verify CA`. (2) **Provision + distribute the CA bundle** (pin §3 Option A) into the prod app container and set `DbTls__SslMode=Verify CA` + `DbTls__CaPath=<path>` in the prod config. If the owner cannot distribute a bundle, record the justification for the `Require` fallback (pin §2) **or** the `Trust Server Certificate` escape (pin §3 Option B) — **do not leave prod at `Prefer`.** (3) **Re-provision on CA rotation** — when the prod addon's server CA rotates, the distributed bundle + `DbTls__CaPath` value must be updated or the `Verify CA` handshake fails. **None of these is assumed done.** F21.U3 records them as the owner's remaining steps before SRV-04 can be re-verified as PASS.
- **F21 is now at U3 (F21.U2 COMPLETE — the env knob).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F21.U3 + F22 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit).   1. **Enable Postgres TLS on the prod Postgres addon** (managed-DB TLS or a self-managed `postgres:18` with a cert) so the server actually *serves* TLS — a client-side `SslMode=Verify CA` against a server that does not offer TLS is a **hard connection failure**, so this must be true **before** the prod value flips from `Prefer` to `Verify CA`.
  2. **Provision + distribute the CA bundle** (pin §3 Option A) into the prod app container and set `DbTls__SslMode=Verify CA` + `DbTls__CaPath=<path>` in the prod config (the two keys the F21.U2 knob reads at `Program.cs` ~92–104). If the owner cannot distribute a bundle, record the justification for the `Require` fallback (pin §2) **or** the `Trust Server Certificate` escape (pin §3 Option B) — **do not leave prod at `Prefer`.**
  3. **Re-provision on CA rotation** — when the prod addon's server CA rotates, the distributed bundle + `DbTls__CaPath` value must be updated or the `Verify CA` handshake fails (the standing cost of pin §3 Option A).
- **Invariants held (this unit + the lane):** **ADR 0004 forward-only + "app role owns the DB"** — **preserved, not touched** (F21 changes *how* the app connects (TLS), **not** ownership / schema / migration; **F22** is the unit that touches ownership). **ADR 0006 module boundaries + Core stays HTTP-free** — **held** (no new seam, no Core type; the knob is a Web `Program.cs` config read). **The F21.U2 knob in `Program.cs`** — **already committed and untouched by this unit** (F21.U3 only records the ops half; it does not edit `Program.cs`). **The baseline `ConnectionStrings:Kumunita` shape** — **extended, not replaced** (unset ⇒ byte-for-byte baseline). **C3 single-write-lane / server-rendered stack** — **untouched** (no write lane, no route, no view).
- **F21 is now COMPLETE (U1–U3).** U1 the pin, U2 the `Program.cs` env knob (code half), U3 this owner-ops close-out (the `code+ops` ops half — recorded, not assumed done). The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **only F22 remains**. **No 6-member close-flip yet** (that happens at **F22**, the lane's last unit). Next unit = **F22** (DB-18: demote the app role — least-privilege; `code+ops`) — a fresh clean-slate agent reads this entry + the pin **§8** (the three recorded open items) + **§2 / §3** (the values + CA the owner must set) + `Program.cs` **~92–104** (the landed knob + the two consumers) — and then proceeds to the F22 pin, which is the unit that touches the **ADR 0004 "app role owns the DB"** ownership question.

## F22.U1 — Part-0 design pin + ADR amendment (FIRST UNIT OF F22 — THE LANE'S LAST FIX)

This unit **proposes** the split + amendment; it does **not** change `dev-db-init/01-app-role.sql`, `Program.cs`, any accepted ADR's status, or any code. **F1–F21 are COMPLETE; F22 is now at U2.**
- **Pins relied on:** `0004-data-persistence-and-schema-evolution.md` **§A** (two schemas, one Postgres; the ownership boundary table) + **§B** (the applied-state contract — *"a pristine database gets its initial state with no operator step"* + *"Re-running an already-applied step is a no-op"*; the "app role owns the DB so it can create the `mt` and `identity` schemas at boot" invariant this fix reconciles). `dev-db-init/01-app-role.sql` (the **current** single-owner DDL — `CREATE DATABASE kumunita OWNER kumunita` — the pin proposes the two-role split against this real DDL). `prod-hardening-f21-db-tls.md` **§8** (the "recorded, not assumed done" voice) + **§9** (the exit-contract / forward-pointer shape a `code+ops` pin uses). `hardening-handoff-notes.md` **F21.U3** (the voice + that **F22 is now the last fix; F21 is COMPLETE**).
- **What this unit did (no code):** F22.U1 is the **Part-0 pin + ADR amendment** of F22, and — because F22 **touches ownership** (unlike F21, which only changed *how* the app connects) — it **records the ADR 0004 tension and proposes the two-role split *before* any later unit proceeds.** The four pinned items: (1) the **two-role split** (§2 — `kumunita_migrate` DB-owner + schema-owner = the DDL role vs. `kumunita` demoted to `CONNECT` + `USAGE` + `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` = the DML role, **no** `DROP`/`CREATE SCHEMA`/`CREATE DATABASE`); (2) the **boot-ownership migration** (§3 + §4 — the 4-step pristine-boot ordering where the DDL step and the DML step run as **different roles**, preserving ADR 0004 §B's no-operator-step boot; the `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` line is the load-bearing piece); (3) the **pinned test name `AppRole_CannotDropSchema`** (§5, **not renamed** — asserts the app role is refused `DROP`/`CREATE SCHEMA` with SQLSTATE `42501` while DML still succeeds); (4) the **invariants held** (§6). The **ADR 0004 tension** is recorded (§1) and the reconciliation is a **proposal** — **ADR 0171, Status: Proposed, gated on owner acceptance** — **not** an accepted change. The **owner-ops open items** are recorded, **not assumed done** (§7).

- **Drift pause:** none. F22.U1 adds its row as **0171** (next after the highest **file** number) and does not touch 0170's missing row.
