## F22.U2 — Drift pause (implementation scope)
- **Gate is now CLEAR:** ADR 0171 = **Accepted** (owner accepted 2026-10-25; `docs/adr/0171-least-privilege-db-role.md` header + `docs/adr/README.md:182` both read `Accepted`; commit `fd2ca6f1`). The earlier ADR-gate pause is **lifted** — this pause is a **different, implementation-scope** blocker (pin vs. codebase), per unit-series **rule 4** ("a unit that finds the codebase diverged from the pin records a Drift pause and stops").
- **What was done before stopping:** all entry reads (pin **§2/§3/§4/§5/§6/§7/§8**, `dev-db-init/01-app-role.sql`, `Program.cs` **~92–104** the F21 `DbTls` knob + **~107/486** the two DML consumers) **plus a full trace of the actual boot-DDL path**. **No code written, no build, no test run** — the split is **not** landable within the pin's stated scope (see below).
- **The divergence (verified, 4 facts):** the pin §3 / §8 says U2 = "a small `DbMigrate` env-knob pair in `Program.cs` (the F21 `DbTls` shape) wired into the boot schema-apply + grant path," **≤2 files / ≤~300 LOC, no Core change**. That assumption does **not** hold against this codebase's architecture, where the **boot DDL and the runtime DML share one DI-registered store**:
  1. The boot DDL (create `mt`/`identity` + tables) is driven by **Core's** `SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync` (`src/Kumunita.Core/Bootstrap/SchemaBootstrap.cs:30`), which resolves the **DI-registered** `IDocumentStore` + `AppDbContext` — **both bound to the single shared app connection** (`Program.cs:107` `opts.Connection(kumunitaConnection)` for Marten; `Program.cs:486` `UseNpgsql(kumunitaConnection)` for EF). There is **no separate "boot DDL seam"** to re-point at a migrate role — the boot path and the per-request DML path are the **same** store.
  2. Wolverine's message-store DDL (`wolverine_*`, in `mt`) runs as the **app role during `app.StartAsync()`** via `IntegrateWithWolverine()` (`Program.cs:318`) — i.e. **before** `SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync` (`Program.cs:998`). A "route boot DDL as `kumunita_migrate`" knob **cannot** intercept this, because it is the same DI store that also serves runtime DML.
  3. The dev-only shape loop `marten.ApplyAllDatabaseChangesOnStartup()` (`Program.cs:327–329`) also emits DDL as the app role.
  4. **Coupling:** landing **deliverable 1** (the `01-app-role.sql` two-role DDL) **in isolation breaks dev boot** — `docker-compose.yml:52` still connects as `Username=kumunita` with **no** `DbMigrate` env, so a fresh `docker compose down -v && up` would run the boot DDL (facts 1–3) as the now-**DML-only** app role → **`42501 insufficient_privilege`**. The two deliverables are **not independently landable**; and the "route the boot DDL as the migrate role" piece that would fix it is **not** a "small env-knob pair" — it requires either a **forbidden Core change** to `SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync` (deliverables forbid any Core change), or a **large second-store + `AppDbContext` + Wolverine re-registration** (≫ "≤2 files / ≤~300 LOC").
- **Why this is a pause and not an implementation:** the pin's stated scope ("≤2 files / ≤~300 LOC, no Core") does **not** cover a *working* split in this architecture. Forcing it would mean either (a) editing **`Kumunita.Core`** (`SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync`) — explicitly out of U2's deliverables — or (b) a much larger second-store re-architecture the pin never sized. Both are owner-level scope decisions on a **high-risk, ADR-gated** change; a clean-slate agent must not silently expand the scope or ship a half-split that breaks dev boot.
- **Decision points for the owner (a re-scope is needed before U2 can proceed — pick one):**
  - **(A) Expand F22.U2's scope** to allow a **keyed second Marten store + a migrate-role `AppDbContext`**, **plus a matching Core change to `SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync`** to run the boot DDL + grants as `kumunita_migrate` (the architecturally-correct path; the runtime DML keeps the existing app-role store). This **changes** the "no Core change / ≤2 files" constraint and needs a **new ADR addendum to 0171** pinning the boot-path mechanism. Then re-run U2.
  - **(B) Split F22 into a design unit + a later implementation unit:** F22.U2 becomes **design-only** (pin the boot-path re-architecture: keyed stores + the `SchemaBootstrap` change + the Wolverine-During-`StartAsync` ordering), and the actual DDL/knob/Core change lands in a **newly-sized, owner-blessed unit**. Safer; keeps each unit atomic + scoped.
  - **(C) Defer the dev-side split:** apply the two-role split **on prod only** (the owner-ops step, F22.U3) against the **existing** single-role `01-app-role.sql`, and **leave the `Program.cs`/dev-compose two-role path for a future, properly-scoped unit** once the boot path is re-architected. (This does **not** close DB-18 as a *code* fix — only the owner's prod demote does — so it is the weakest option.)
- **Test result:** **no code, no build, no test run** (the `AppRole_CannotDropSchema` test is **pinned, not written** — it lands with a re-scoped U2; the fixture's `kumunita` role in `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/PostgresFixture.cs` is a Testcontainers admin user, so the test's "app role is DML-only" precondition must be established by the re-scoped unit, not assumed).
- **Invariants held (this unit):** **no change to any `src/**` / `tests/**` / `dev-db-init/**` file, no build, no test run** — ADR 0004, ADR 0006, backward-compat, and the C3/server-rendered invariants are all **untouched** (nothing was edited).
- **Open items (owner, recorded — NOT assumed done):** unchanged from F22.U1 §7 **plus** the new **re-scope decision (A/B/C above)**. The three prod steps — (1) provision `kumunita_migrate` + make it DB/schema owner **before** demoting the app role; (2) demote the app role to DML (apply §4 step-3 grants, revoke owner/DDL, verify `42501` + DML green first); (3) set the `DbMigrate` env — remain the substance of **F22.U3** and are **not** runnable until U2's code lands, which is **blocked on the re-scope decision**.
- **F22 is still at U2 (blocked on a re-scope decision).** The prod-hardening lane stays **open** — **F22.U2 + F22.U3 remain**. **No 6-member close-flip yet** (that happens at **F22.U3**, the lane's last unit, **after** a re-scoped U2 lands the split). **Next step:** the owner picks **A / B / C**; the next clean-slate agent re-runs **F22.U2** under the chosen scope. Read order: this entry + pin **§2/§3/§4** (the role matrix, boot ordering, DDL) + **§5** (test shape) + **§6** (invariants) + **§7** (owner ops) + `SchemaBootstrap.cs` (the boot-DDL seam the re-scope must route) + `Program.cs:107/318/327–329/486/998` (the shared-store + Wolverine + dev-loop + seeder call sites) + `docker-compose.yml:52` (the dev boot dependency that couples the two deliverables).

## F22.U2 — implementation (owner chose **Option A**; ADR 0171 = Accepted)
- **Owner decision (2026-10-25):** re-scope per **Option A** — allow the **Core change** (`SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync`) + a **new ADR addendum to 0171**, and *"Go ahead with Design A, I prefer a stricter adherence to security best practices"* → the **strict** split (the app role is **not** the DB owner, owns **no** schema, and is refused `CREATE SCHEMA` / `DROP SCHEMA` / `DROP DATABASE`). **Backward-compat for existing deployments de-prioritized** — owner: *"All production deployments are demos only … I don't mind having to install them from scratch"* → no live-DB `ALTER … OWNER` migration path is authored; the split is simply what provisioning produces.
- **Landed (6 files — `git status` of the uncommitted tree):** (1) `dev-db-init/01-app-role.sql` — two-role DDL (`kumunita_migrate` owns the DB + `mt`; `kumunita` granted `CONNECT` only); (2) `dev-db-init/02-app-schemas.sh` (NEW) — pre-creates `mt AUTHORIZATION kumunita_migrate` + `GRANT USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA mt TO kumunita` against the **app DB** (a `.sh`, because Postgres runs `.sql` init against the `postgres` DB, not `kumunita`); (3) `src/Kumunita.Core/Bootstrap/SchemaBootstrap.cs` — `ApplyAsync(…, string? migrateConnection = null, …)` runs EF `MigrateAsync` **as `kumunita_migrate`** + issues the identity grants (and degrades to the single-role baseline when the key is unset); (4) `src/Kumunita.Web/Program.cs` — reads `ConnectionStrings:KumunitaMigrate` and passes it to `SchemaBootstrap.ApplyAsync`; (5) `docker-compose.yml` — `ConnectionStrings__KumunitaMigrate` env on the app; (6) `tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/F22_AppRoleCannotDropSchemaTests.cs` (NEW) — the `AppRole_CannotDropSchema` witness. **Plus the ADR 0171 "Implementation" addendum** (below). **Deviation from the pin, recorded:** the env key is **`ConnectionStrings:KumunitaMigrate`**, **not** the pin's placeholder `DbMigrate`.
- **The one forced exception (the pin's "owns no schema" held precisely):** the app role keeps **schema-level `CREATE` on `mt`** (not ownership) because Wolverine + Marten create `mt.wolverine_*` / `mt.mt_*` **as the app role** during `app.StartAsync()` — the strict DDL demotion (no DB-owner, no schema-owner, no `CREATE/DROP SCHEMA`, no `DROP DATABASE`) is fully enforced on top of it. Verified: the app role **can** `CREATE TABLE mt.x` + `INSERT` while `DROP SCHEMA mt` / `CREATE SCHEMA` / `DROP DATABASE` are all refused (`42501`).
- **Test result — the `AppRole_CannotDropSchema` witness (Testcontainers):** GREEN — `Total: 1, Passed: 1, Failed: 0, Skipped: 0, Exit Code: 0`. It asserts the app role is refused `CREATE SCHEMA` / `DROP SCHEMA` / `DROP DATABASE` (`42501`) while `CREATE TABLE mt.f22_probe` + `INSERT` + `SELECT count` succeed, and `kumunita_migrate` (DB owner) can `CREATE SCHEMA`. (Helper detail that mattered: `SwapDb` sets **`Pooling=false`** — pooled connections otherwise hold the scratch DB open and teardown fails with `55006`.)
- **Test result — full `Kumunita.Core.Tests` (post-`ON TABLES`-fix):** **GREEN — `Total: 1562, Errors: 0, Failed: 0, Skipped: 2`** (includes the new `AppRole_CannotDropSchema` witness class; run via `dotnet exec tests/Kumunita.Core.Tests/bin/Debug/net10.0/Kumunita.Core.Tests.dll`, `test_exit=0`).
- **Pristine-boot end-to-end evidence (the rendered/running system is the evidence — `docker compose down -v && up`):** ownership `db_owner=kumunita_migrate`, `schema_owner(mt)=kumunita_migrate`, `schema_owner(identity)=kumunita_migrate`; the app role's standing is `mt_usage=true mt_create=true identity_usage=true` + full DML on `identity."AspNetUsers"` (`select/insert/update/delete=true`) + `post_select=true` + `aspnet_users_count=9` (seeded); `wolverine_incoming_envelopes_owner=kumunita` (the app created it) vs `AspNetUsers_owner=kumunita_migrate` (EF ran as the migrate role); negatives all refused (`DROP SCHEMA mt` → "must be owner of schema"; `CREATE SCHEMA f22x` → "permission denied for database"; `DROP DATABASE` → "must be owner of database"); positive `CREATE TABLE mt.f22_probe` + `INSERT` + `count=2`. **The app role is the DB *role*, the migrate role is the DB *owner*** — DB-18's invariant is now true.
- **Bug found + fixed during verification:** the identity-grant `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … GRANT … TO kumunita` was **missing the required `ON TABLES`** clause → `42601: syntax error at or near "TO"`, and because the whole grant batch is one implicit transaction, that **aborted the `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA identity` + table grants too** — which is why the first boot left `identity` invisible to `kumunita` and the app crashed on an unhandled exception. Added `ON TABLES`; the second pristine boot came up clean and fully granted. (A second, **non-app** red herring in the boot script was `has_schema_privilege('kumunita','identity','SELECT')` — `SELECT` is a *table* privilege, not a schema one; corrected to `has_table_privilege` for `identity."AspNetUsers"`.)
- **Pins relied on:** pin **§2** (the two-role matrix), **§3** (boot ownership), **§4** (the exact DDL + the `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` load-bearing line), **§5** (the `AppRole_CannotDropSchema` shape — **not renamed**), **§6** (the invariants), **§7** (the owner-ops open items); **ADR 0004 §A/§B** (the amended ownership + the pristine-signal / forward-only contract the split must preserve); **ADR 0171** (now Accepted + the new Implementation addendum); `dev-db-init/01-app-role.sql` (the DDL this unit replaced) + `02-app-schemas.sh` (NEW).
- **Invariants held (this unit + the lane):** **ADR 0004 forward-only + delta-detected idempotent boot + "pristine database gets its initial state with no operator step" — PRESERVED** (the app still comes up from a bare DB with zero operator steps; the *capability* to create the `identity` schema simply moved to `kumunita_migrate`, which the app invokes at boot). **The "app role owns the DB" invariant — RECONCILED by ADR 0171** (Accepted), not dropped. **ADR 0006 module boundaries + Core stays HTTP-free — HELD** (the only Core edit is a `Bootstrap` config/privilege pass-through, no new seam, no cross-context call, no `<PackageReference>`, no EF migration). **Backward compat — HELD** (the unset-`KumunitaMigrate` path degrades to the single-role baseline — the `Program.cs` `if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(migrateConnection))` branch). **C3 single-write-lane / server-rendered — UNTOUCHED** (no route, no view, no write lane).
- **Open items (owner, recorded — NOT assumed done):** the three **pin §7** prod steps, unchanged in substance, now with the **real** env key — (1) **provision `kumunita_migrate`** on the prod Postgres + make it the owner of the DB + the `mt`/`identity` schemas **before** demoting the app role; (2) **demote the prod app role to DML** (it keeps `mt` schema-level `CREATE` — the forced exception — but loses DB/`identity` ownership + `CREATE/DROP SCHEMA` + `DROP DATABASE`, verified `42501` + DML green first); (3) **set `ConnectionStrings:KumunitaMigrate`** on the prod app (the real key — **not** the pin's `DbMigrate` placeholder). If the owner cannot provision a second role on the managed addon, **record the justification — do not leave the app role as the database owner** while claiming least-privilege.
- **F22 is now at U3 — the lane's LAST unit.** U1 the pin, U2 this implementation (code + ADR addendum + witness, all green + pristine-boot-verified). **No 6-member close-flip yet** — that happens at **F22.U3**. ## 2026-10-25 verification + fix pass (F6 / F7 / F8)

A code-vs-docs pass over the closed lane (per the plan's "next audit run can
diff against this one" contract) found **19 of 22** rows genuinely closed
(code + test + docs all present and green) but **three rows the `## Summary`
had marked as handled were not actually closed**, and the close-flip docs
repeated the over-claim. The three, and their disposition now:

- **F7 (WEB-07)** — the first pass claimed "the CSP middleware already ships
  the two headers" — **false**. `Program.cs`'s only global-header middleware
  was the CSP block; `nosniff` was set per-controller on serve/download lanes
  only, and `Referrer-Policy` was **nowhere** in `src/`. **Fixed:** the two
  headers are now set **globally** in the same `app.Use(…)` CSP block (the
  per-controller `nosniff` stays — this is the global floor).
- **F8 (WEB-10)** — the first pass claimed "the upload lanes already ride the
  existing per-IP rate-limit policies" — **false**. None of the four upload
  POSTs carried `[EnableRateLimiting]`; the `IUploadGate` is a *size/quota*
  guard, not a *rate* guard. (Notably F9.U2 had already fixed this exact bug
  class for signup.) **Fixed:** a new `upload` policy (10 / 15 min per IP) in
  the `AddRateLimiter` block, attached via `[EnableRateLimiting("upload")]` to
  all four lanes (`/attachment`, `/content-image`, `/documents`,
  `/profile/avatar`) — the same one-line-per-action pattern F9.U2 established.
- **F6 (AUTH-09)** — the first pass claimed F6 was "superseded by the F2 SHA-256
  token-hash lane" — **unsound**: F2 hashes one-time verify/setup tokens, not
  passwords; no `AddPasswordHasher` / BCrypt / Argon2 exists in `src/`. The
  correct disposition is that the platform's own `SECURITY.md` §6 (2026-08-27)
  already **deliberately decided** PBKDF2-at-Identity-default + "no custom
  password scheme." **Recorded as ADR 0172** (Accepted, no code change) so the
  audit row and that decision now agree, instead of the row dangling as a
  false "done."

  `app.Use(…)` block; the `upload` fixed-window policy (F8) in the
